    16	## The answer first
    17	
    18	None of the three caches is needed today. `project-memory/` does not exist yet, so nothing has been measured on it.
    19	My reading of the research is that at the sizes measured elsewhere, `INDEX.md`, grep and git answer the questions
    20	each cache would answer. Each cache below has a
    21	**trigger**: a condition that can be observed and that makes it worth building. None of them is built before its
    22	trigger fires.
    23	
    24	This follows the design's own rule: `.cache/` is optional, gitignored and rebuilt from the files, and its absence
    25	must not stop anyone reading memory (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`; recommendation M4, M6). It also follows the repo's own
    26	history of building graphs nobody queried (`docs/KNOWLEDGE_GRAPH_DESIGN.md` §1, quoted in `G-F21`).
    27	
    28	## Measured here: development and testing
    29	
    30	EJ's aim is that development and testing get faster and cheaper in tokens. So this section measures all three stores
    31	against grep on **this repo**: 490 files (123 `.py`, 367 `.md`, 4.8 MB), with 40 questions an agent asks while
    32	developing and testing it. Run: `runs/20261012-caches-routing/` (results in `bench/results.md`, ids `BM-*` below).
    33	
    34	**Who wrote each check, and what it does not establish.**
    35	- The **questions and answers** were written by codex (`gpt-6.1-sol`), each checked by an `rg` command it ran. Five
    36	  Claude agents re-checked all 40: 37 were correct and 3 were incomplete. Those 3 were completed.
    37	- **"Which tests run this code"** is also scored against per-test runtime coverage (`pytest-cov`, no model). That
    38	  counts in-process Python only, so it misses tests that run the code as a separate process.
    39	- The **harness** was written by opencode. A Claude audit found 5 high-severity biases, and a Claude repair fixed
    40	  them; the repair was **not re-audited**. Rebuilding from scratch gave the same answers on all 180 question-route
    41	  pairs.
    42	- **Token cost** is measured as *characters printed up to the first line that names a right file*. That is a proxy,
    43	  not a token count, and a weak one:
    44	  - it stops at the right *file*, not the right answer;
    45	  - misses are left out, so each route's mean is over a different set of questions;
    46	  - each route prints a different format.
    47	
    48	  No agent was run end to end.
    49	- One repo, one question set, **n = 6 to 10 per category**. None of this is a project-memory corpus.
    50	
    51	**What each route returns, per category** (symbol hit in the top 5 / characters read to the first right answer):
    52	
    53	| | grep (`rg`) | SQLite full-text | vector | hybrid | code graph |
    54	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    55	| **BM-1** exact identifier (8) | 0.75 / 920 | 0.63 / 356 | 0.0 / 837 | 0.5 / 91 | **0.75 / 43** |
    56	| **BM-2** concept in other words (8) | 0 / — | 0 / — | 0 (file 0.25) / 955 | 0 (file 0.13) / 1,427 | n/a |
    57	| **BM-3** structure and test impact (10) | 0.8 / 1,015 | **0.9 / 175** | 0.1 / 1,421 | 0.9 / 362 | 0.7 / **61** |
    58	| **BM-4** decision or reason in the docs (8) | 0.63 / 4,470 | **0.63 / 525** | 0.5 / 810 | 0.63 / 516 | n/a |
    59	| **BM-5** how to run or configure (6) | 0.17 / 6,875 | 0.33 / 1,146 | 0.5 / 569 | 0.5 / 1,120 | n/a |
    60	
    61	- **BM-6, all categories.** Characters printed up to the first right file (each mean over that route's hits only:
    62	  grep 26, full-text 25, vector 19, hybrid 26, graph 13): grep 2,688, full-text 451, hybrid 528, vector
    63	  923, graph 53 (graph over the 20 questions that name a symbol or file). Total output per answer: grep 4,304,
    64	  full-text 1,817.
    65	- **BM-7, test impact against runtime coverage** (C01–C04, n=4). The share of the tests that really run the function
    66	  each route found: graph 0.78; grep, full-text and hybrid 0.59; vector 0.04.
    67	- **BM-8, tests that run the code as a separate process** (C05–C07). The graph returns **nothing**, because no
    68	  call edge exists. Full-text search on the file name found 2 of 3; grep found 1 of 3 and printed up to 8.7K
    69	  characters.
    70	- **BM-9, speed.** Every route answers in at most 15 ms per question; the slowest was grep on E04, at 14.7 ms. Full rebuilds take 0.17 s for full-text (9.5 MB),
    71	  0.94 s for vector (10.2 MB) and 0.89 s for the graph (4.9 MB). The full test suite, 529 tests, takes 31–33 s (3
    72	  runs).
    73	
    74	**What the numbers say (my reading).**
    75	1. **On this repo, speed is not the reason to use a cache.** Every route takes milliseconds (BM-9). The difference is
    76	   in **what the route prints**: full-text prints about a sixth of grep's characters before naming a right file
    77	   (BM-6). Whether that becomes a sixth of an agent's tokens is not measured. Where
    78	   the question names a symbol or file, the graph gets there in tens of characters (BM-1, BM-3); its BM-6 mean is over
    79	   those 20 questions only, so it is not a like-for-like ratio.
    80	2. **The vector index loses almost everywhere it was tried.** It never found an exact identifier (BM-1), and it found
    81	   4% of the tests that run a function (BM-7). Its only edge is "how do I run or configure X" (BM-5, n=6), which is
    82	   too small to act on. Nothing here contradicts the earlier rule that vectors come last and only as a hybrid.
    83	3. **No route answers a concept asked in other words in one shot** (BM-2). That still takes an agent searching and
    84	   reading. It is exactly the miss the vector trigger is meant to record.
    85	4. **No single store answers "which tests should I run".** The graph finds tests that reach the code indirectly
    86	   (BM-7), but is blind to tests that run it as a separate process (BM-8). Full-text search on the file name finds
    87	   the latter. An empty graph answer means *not seen*, never *no tests*. Even combined, the two routes
    88	   can miss affected tests: both missed C05 entirely, and the graph found only 0.33 and 0.8 of the runtime tests for
    89	   C01 and C02 (BM-7). That is why the full suite runs before committing.
    90	
    91	**Routing rule for development and testing (proposed).**
    92	
    93	| the question | use | then | evidence |
    94	|---|---|---|---|
    95	| Where is function or method X defined? | code graph | read that definition | BM-1 |
    96	| Where is a flag, constant or string defined? | full-text, or grep | read the hit | BM-1 (A05) |
    97	| What calls X? | code graph | read the callers | BM-3 (C08–C10: 33–46 characters) |
    98	| Which tests should I run after changing function X or file F? | code graph (tests reaching X or F) **plus** full-text on the file name (tests that run it as a separate process) | run that subset; run the full suite (31–33 s) before committing | BM-7, BM-8, BM-9 |
    99	| Why did we decide X? Where is X explained in the docs? | full-text (ties hybrid on hits; prints less in total) | read the section | BM-4 |
   100	| How do I run or configure X? | not settled: hybrid and vector scored 0.5 and full-text 0.33, on 6 questions | read the hit | BM-5 (n=6, too small to act on) |
   101	| A concept, in my own words | grep and read, as an agent search | record a miss if the answer is found another way | BM-2 |
   102	| Anything the graph answers with nothing | full-text, then grep | — | BM-8 |
   103	
   104	This table is for the **code and docs**. Memory entries are covered by the per-store sections below. The two
   105	differ because the corpus differs. Code and docs exist today and are already large enough that full-text and the
   106	graph save reading (BM-6). `project-memory/` does not exist yet.
   107	
   108	**What this does not change, and what it proposes.**
   109	- **Unchanged:** the rules for all three caches below (full rebuild, rebuild when stale, fall back when absent) and
   110	  R1–R8 apply as written.
   111	- **Conflict with "The answer first" and the vision:** for code and docs, the measurements say full-text and the graph
   112	  already save reading today. That contradicts "none of the three caches is needed today" and the vision's *who it is
   113	  for: an agent retrieving an entry*. Changing either is EJ's call. The proposed wording is in
   114	  `runs/20261012-caches-routing/vision-proposals.md`, and neither file has been edited.
   115	
   116	## Rules for all three caches
   117	
   118	These apply to whichever cache is built. They come from how the researched tools fail.
   119	
   120	- **Full rebuild, never patched.** A live, incrementally updated index drifted silently from a clean rebuild: 4.3% of
   121	  edges wrong, with every integrity check passing (`G-F5 drift`). Its makers decided drift cannot be measured cheaply
   122	  (`G-F5c`). A full rebuild of this repo's code graph takes 0.94 s (`G-F12`); an FTS rebuild of 10,000 synthetic
   123	  entries takes 0.53 s (`S-F13`).
   124	- **Rebuild before answering when stale.** Before each query, compare the newest source file with the index and
   125	  rebuild first if the source is newer. Build into a temporary file and rename it over the old one (`S-F9`, quoted:
   126	  *"A stale index is worse than no index."*).
   127	- **Fall back when absent.** If the cache file or SQLite's FTS5 is missing, the tool warns once and answers with grep
   128	  (`S-F9`); the same for a missing embedding model (my reading, extending `S-F9`). A mistyped database path silently
   129	  creates an empty database, which looks exactly like a stale one (`G-F5 drift`, failure mode 5), so the tool opens the
   130	  cache read-only and treats "no such file" as absent.
   131	- **Index only active entries**, the same set as `INDEX.md`, with `status` and `covers` stored beside each row
   132	  (`V-F22`, my reading). Otherwise superseded entries come back looking current.
   133

 succeeded in 0ms:
   207	## SQLite full-text index
   208	
   209	### Five whys
   210	
   211	1. **Why a full-text index?** To find entries by their words, ranked, with a snippet, instead of grep's unranked list
   212	   (`S-F1`, quoted: *"grep returns unranked matches."*).
   213	2. **Why does ranking matter?** Every hit the agent opens costs context. In the one controlled study, BM25 used 5.8K
   214	   tokens per question against 226K for an agent using grep over files, on its smallest corpus: 1,144 documents,
   215	   1.7M tokens (`S-F4`).
   216	3. **Why not build it now?** That study puts the point where BM25 overtakes the grep agent at around 10 million tokens
   217	   of text, and the grep agent led at the smallest sizes (`S-F4`). At our entry size that is about 37,000 entries
   218	   (`S-F5`, my reading via its 4-characters-per-token assumption). `INDEX.md` already lists every active entry. In the
   219	   researcher's own test, at 10,000 synthetic entries a one-word grep took 0.038 s and a grep chain for a header
   220	   question 0.06 s; at 50,000 the header chain took 0.15 s, and the one-word timing was invalid (`S-F13`). No study
   221	   covers a corpus of a few hundred to a few thousand short entries (`S-F5`: not measured).
   222	4. **Why might it be needed later?** `INDEX.md` stops being enough once it can no longer list every active entry under
   223	   its size limit. The agent then loses the one-line scan of every entry, and ranked search is what replaces it (my
   224	   reading).
   225	5. **Why only as a cache?** The markdown is the source of truth, and the cache must be rebuildable and skippable (M6).
   226	
   227	Structured questions such as "every active decision covering path X" or "what supersedes Y" do not need SQLite. A
   228	short script reading the entry headers answers them in 0.1 s at 10,000 entries (`S-F13`). That measures time, not
   229	tokens; my reading is that the script and an SQL query return the same list of entries, so the agent reads the same
   230	amount either way (not measured).
   231	
   232	### 5W1H
   233	
   234	| | |
   235	|---|---|
   236	| **Who** | Built and rebuilt by a script with no model call. Read by any agent on demand. EJ decides to build it when the trigger fires. |
   237	| **What** | One FTS5 table with `kind`, `status` and `covers` as `UNINDEXED` columns (`S-F2`). One row per active entry file: the research leaves the row grain open (`S-F17`); my reading is that an entry of one or two sentences is already one section. The default `unicode61` tokenizer: in the researcher's own one-row test, a plausible change to keep paths whole made ordinary queries return zero rows silently, and ids such as `D-0012` had to be quoted (`S-F16`). |
   238	| **When** | Built when the trigger fires: `INDEX.md` can no longer list every active entry. Rebuilt before any query that finds it stale. Never read at session start. |
   239	| **Where** | `project-memory/.cache/index.sqlite`, gitignored. |
   240	| **Why** | The five whys above. |
   241	| **How** | One stdlib `sqlite3` script, the smallest option in the SQLite node (§5, option B): rebuild to a temporary file and rename; compare modification times for staleness; fall back to `rg`. Nothing to install: SQLite 3.45.1 with FTS5 works here (verified 2026-10-12). |
   242	
   243	## Vector index
   244	
   245	### Five whys
   246	
   247	1. **Why a vector index?** To find an entry by meaning when the question shares no words with it.
   248	2. **Why can't grep or full-text search do that?** They match words. But the evidence does not show vectors winning on
   249	   memory text. (They do win on some code tasks, `V-F5`, and on half the file-based pairs in `V-F3`.) On conversational
   250	   memory, grep returned inline beat vector search for every harness and model pair (`V-F3`). On a 500,000-document
   251	   corpus, BM25 beat vectors even on the questions built to favour them (`V-F4`). On decision-style records, a
   252	   structured graph and a vector memory tool were "largely equivalent" on relevance, and plain notes search did no
   253	   better on listing or supersession questions (s3-F34; `V-F1`).
   254	3. **Why not build it now?** No measurement exists on a corpus shaped like `entries/` (`V-F11`: not measured).
   255	   Anthropic's 2024 guidance is that under about 200,000 tokens a knowledge base can simply be put in the prompt
   256	   (`V-F8`), roughly 400–650 entries (`V-F12`, my reading). This design does not do that (it never loads the whole
   257	   tree), so this is only a bound: below it, retrieval quality is not what limits an agent (my reading). Vectors also
   258	   add failures of their own: negation (`V-F20`), exact ids and paths (`V-F21`), and a second rebuild step that can lag
   259	   the first (`V-F24`).
   260	4. **Why might it be needed later?** If agents keep missing entries that exist because their words differ from the
   261	   entry's. The trigger is a record of such misses: the entry existed, `INDEX.md`, grep and full-text search all missed
   262	   it, and it was found another way (`V-F13`, my reading). How many misses justify building it is a **measured comparison** (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` § How each threshold is
   263	found), not a number to guess. No
   264	   source gives one.
   265	5. **Why hybrid only?** On memory-style text, the positive results found combine vectors with keyword search (`V-F6`,
   266	   `V-F7`; `V-F10` is unproven), and vectors alone were the weakest arm when results were returned inline (`V-F3`,
   267	   `V-F4`). So the vector index comes after the full-text index, never
   268	   instead of it.
   269	
   270	### 5W1H
   271	
   272	| | |
   273	|---|---|
   274	| **Who** | Built and rebuilt by a script with no model call except the embedding model itself. Read by any agent on demand, through the same command as full-text search. EJ decides to build it when the trigger fires. |
   275	| **What** | One vector per active entry file, with the entry's `status`, `covers`, content hash and embedding-model id stored beside it (`V-F24`: a changed model makes every vector useless). Results merged with the full-text results by reciprocal rank fusion (vector node §Q5, from qmd's README). |
   276	| **When** | After the full-text index exists, and after the logged misses outweigh the cache's cost. Rebuilt with the full-text index, in the same step, so the two cannot disagree (`V-F24`, my reading). |
   277	| **Where** | `project-memory/.cache/`, beside `index.sqlite`, gitignored. |
   278	| **Why** | The five whys above. |
   279	| **How** | The smallest options in `V-F18`/`V-F19`: a static Model2Vec model with numpy (option A, no GPU) or `bge-small-en-v1.5` through FastEmbed (option B). Both are small next to the 16 GB GPU here. Embedding speed on this GPU is **not measured** (`V-F18`). No embedding runtime is installed today; numpy is (`V-F19`). |
   280	
   281	## Code graph
   282	
   283	### Five whys
   284	
   285	1. **Why a code graph?** To answer structural questions about the code: what an edit touches, and whether the code an
   286	   entry covers has changed.
   287	2. **Why would memory need that?** For the drift check (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` line 75: mark a decision or fact whose
   288	   code changed with no earlier change to the entry) and for "which entries cover the files I am editing".
   289	3. **Why is git enough for both?** `covers:` names **paths**, not symbols, and is already a hand-kept index from entry
   290	   to path (`G-F14`). Two `git log` calls compare the entry's last change with its paths' last change, in 0.00–0.01 s
   291	   on this repo (`G-F12`; code-graph node §6). Git is the record itself, so it cannot drift.
   292	4. **Why not add a graph for precision anyway?** An incrementally synced graph adds a silent failure that git does not
   293	   have (`G-F5 drift`, 4.3% of edges wrong). A full-rebuild graph such as the repo's own does not drift (`G-F18`), but
   294	   it covers Python only, so for other `covers:` paths it gives a silent partial answer (code-graph node §5, option B).
   295	   The precision it buys, a one-line comment flagging a whole file, can be had by parsing only the changed file with
   296	   Python's `ast` (code-graph node §5, option E). The deeper question, "is this entry still true", belongs to the
   297	   background subagent that compares entries with the code (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` lines 68–73), not to a model-free
   298	   script.
   299	5. **Why might it be needed later?** Any one of the three conditions in the code-graph node's §6, each a test:
   300	   - `covers:` starts naming functions or classes instead of files;
   301	   - the check must answer about files not on any `covers:` list ("what does this change affect");
   302	   - path-level flags raise too many false alarms — the comparison is in `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` § How each threshold
   303	     is found. How many you will tolerate is the one policy value, and it is yours.
   304	
   305	### 5W1H
   306	
   307	| | |
   308	|---|---|
   309	| **Who** | The drift-check script, with no model call. EJ decides to build a graph if one of the three conditions holds. |
   310	| **What** | Today: no graph. Git on `covers:` paths, plus an `ast` parse of changed Python files if precision is needed. If a condition holds: the repo's own `code_graph/code_graph.py` (Python only, deterministic, full rebuild, no incremental mode, `G-F18`) before any outside tool. |
   311	| **When** | Today the check runs whenever `INDEX.md` is generated (my reading of `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` line 75). A graph, if ever built, is rebuilt in full each time it is used. |
   312	| **Where** | Nothing today. Later, `project-memory/.cache/code-graph.json`, gitignored. |
   313	| **Why** | The five whys above. |
   314	| **How** | Two `git log` calls per entry: `git log -1 --format=%at -- <entry file>` and the same for each `covers:` path (code-graph node §6). External indexers (codegraph) are not adopted: their win is measured on source-code architecture questions, not on memory (`G-F7`, `G-F14`), and they bring the drift in `G-F5 drift`. |
   315	
   316	## What this changes elsewhere (proposed, not done)
   317	
   318	- `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` lists as undecided "how the script tells that the entry changed earlier than the code". The
   319	  research suggests an answer for EJ to decide: compare `git log` times of the entry file and its `covers:` paths.
   320	- `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` line 104 can keep `.cache/` as written. This document says when each part of it is built.
   321	
   322	## Not known
   323	
   324	- Every trigger number is EJ's: the `INDEX.md` size limit (already open in `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`), the number of
   325	  recorded misses for vectors, and the false-alarm rate for the code graph.
   326	- No measurement exists of any of the three on a project-memory corpus. All evidence is on other corpora: source code,
   327	  chat memory, enterprise documents.
   328	- The researchers' web reads went through extracts. `S-F*` quotes are not byte-checked (`sqlite-fts.claude.md`,
   329	  header). `V-F*` quotes were checked against saved copies.
   330	- The planted "tool that does not exist" check in the research was void: the names were readable in the run's
   331	  `state.md` (see that file's log).

   333	---
   334	
   335	## Core requirements
   336	
   337	Acceptance criteria are checkable or they are questions for EJ. Where the research gives no number, the criterion
   338	carries `OPEN:` rather than an invented value.
   339	
   340	### R1 — Derived and disposable
   341	The cache is computed from the markdown and never holds knowledge of its own (M4, M6).
   342	
   343	- **R1.1** Deleting the cache and rebuilding from the files restores every answer. *Example:* `rm -rf
   344	  project-memory/.cache/` → run the documented rebuild → the same query returns the same entries. (M6)
   345	- **R1.2** Memory is fully readable with no cache present. *Example:* with `.cache/` absent, session start and an
   346	  on-demand `grep` over `entries/` both work. (M6)
   347	- **R1.3** The cache directory is ignored by git and a rebuild leaves `git status --short` clean. (M6)
   348	
   349	### R2 — Full rebuild, never patched
   350	- **R2.1** The documented rebuild regenerates the whole index from the files; no incremental update path exists.
   351	  *Example:* the rebuild command has no "update" mode. (G-F5 drift)
   352	- **R2.2** The index records what it was built from, so a stale one is detectable. *Example:* the index stores the
   353	  newest source file's mtime or the commit, and the query tool compares before answering. (S-F9)
   354	
   355	### R3 — Stale never answers
   356	- **R3.1** Before answering, the tool compares the newest source file with the index and rebuilds first if the
   357	  source is newer, building into a temporary file and renaming over the old one. (S-F9)
   358	- **R3.2** A wrong path is never mistaken for an empty index: the cache is opened read-only and "no such file" is
   359	  treated as absent. *Example:* point the tool at a typo'd path → it warns and falls back to grep, it does not
   360	  create an empty database. (G-F5, failure mode 5)
   361	
   362	### R4 — Absence degrades, it does not fail
   363	- **R4.1** A missing cache file or a missing FTS5 warns once and answers with grep. (S-F9)
   364	- **R4.2** A missing embedding model does the same. *(my reading, extending S-F9.)*
   365	
   366	### R5 — Nothing is built before its trigger fires
   367	Each cache has an observable trigger. This is the requirement that keeps the whole layer honest. The query-time
   368	counterpart — which store to reach for once a trigger has fired — is the section **When to use which store** above.
   369	
   370	- **R5.1 Full-text** — built only when `INDEX.md` can no longer list every active entry under its size limit.
   371	  `OPEN: not measured — the limit is the comparison in SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md § How each threshold is found` (S-F5, S-F13: not measured for a corpus of a few hundred to a few thousand
   372	  short entries).
   373	- **R5.2 Vector** — built only after R5.1 has fired *and* the logged misses outweigh the cache's cost. A "miss" is
   374	  recorded when an entry existed and `INDEX.md`, grep and full-text search all missed it (V-F13).
   375	  `OPEN: not measured — the miss count is the comparison in SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md § How each threshold is found.` The vector index is hybrid only — appended to
   376	  full-text results by reciprocal rank fusion, never instead of them (V-F3, V-F4, V-F6, V-F7; V-F10 is unproven).
   377	- **R5.3 Code graph** — built only when one of three conditions holds: `covers:` starts naming functions or classes
   378	  rather than files; the check must answer about files on no `covers:` list; or path-level flags raise too many
   379	  false alarms. `OPEN: not measured — the false-alarm comparison is in SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md § How each threshold is found.` (code-graph §6, G-F14, G-F18)
   380	
   381	### R6 — Only active entries, with their status
   382	- **R6.1** The index holds the same set of entries as `INDEX.md`, each row carrying `status` and `covers`.
   383	  *Example:* a superseded entry never appears in a search result as if current. (V-F22)
   384	
   385	### R7 — An answer names its entry
   386	- **R7.1** Every result prints the entry file it came from, so a human can open the source before acting.
   387	  *Example:* search output names `entries/D-0012-*.md`. (M19)
   388	
   389	### R8 — The rebuild stays cheap
   390	- **R8.1** The documented rebuild completes without a model call, except the embedding step of R5.2.
   391	  *Example:* rebuild with no model credentials available; the FTS and code-graph paths still work. (M4, M6)
   392	- **R8.2** `OPEN: not measured — the budget is the comparison in SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md § How each threshold is found.` Measured, for orientation only: 0.94 s (code graph,
   393	  this repo, G-F12) and 0.53 s (FTS, 10,000 synthetic entries, S-F13). Nothing is measured on a real
   394	  `project-memory/` corpus, because it does not exist yet.

+## When to use which store
+
+At query time the memory is answered by the files — `INDEX.md`, grep, git, or reading the entry — and a cache is
+never the answer to a question the files answer. The table below merges three engines asked independently, one store
+each: `claude` on the code graph, `codex` on the vector index, `opencode` on SQLite FTS5. The full 21-row table and
+the 30-row cost table, with every disagreement kept, are in `runs/20261012-caches-enhance/DECISION-RULES.md` — the
+run record. **This file is the live copy.**
+
+**The rule.** **SQLite FTS5** is for a free-text question whose words are likely in the entry text, once `INDEX.md`
+can no longer list every active entry under its size limit and ranked results with snippets save opened files
+(`S-F1`, `S-F4`). **The vector index** is for the same discovery when the question's wording differs from the
+entry's, once the documented miss trigger has fired (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`). **The code graph** is only for a
+multi-hop structural question about Python code, after its trigger, and only as a lower bound (`G-F8`, `G-F17`,
+`G-F18`, `M5`). Everything else is the files.
+
+| the question | store | if not, this | evidence |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| What do we already know about X? (free text, words likely in the entry) | **FTS5** — conditional on the size trigger | `INDEX.md`, grep, then read | S-F1, S-F4 |
+| Which prior entry matches when my words differ from its? (find by meaning) | **vector** — conditional on the miss trigger | `INDEX.md`, grep, then read | V-F11, V-F13, M4 |
+| What does changing F reach, several hops out, including files on no `covers:` list? | **code graph** — after its trigger; Python only; lower bound | grep imports and callers, then read | G-F8, G-F17, G-F18 |
+| Which tests reach function F, so I know what to run first? | **code graph** — conditional, lower bound | grep test names, then run the suite | G-F12, R5.3 |
+| An exact identifier, an error string, a known `file:line` | none | literal grep or filename lookup, then read | V-F7, V-F21, s3-F29 |
+| Every active entry covering path X — completeness | none — a ranked top-k cannot prove completeness | exhaustive scan of `INDEX.md` and entry headers | V-F21 |
+| Proving absence ("have we never recorded…") | none — no hit is not proof | exhaustive search, then read | V-F20 |
+| A structured header question (status, `supersedes`, `depends_on`) | none | `INDEX.md` scan or a script over frontmatter; follow links by grep | V-F22, M13 |
+| Why did we choose this, or why is this line like this? | none | the entry's **Why:** and its evidence; `git blame`, `git log -S` | s3-F4 |
+| Did the covered code change after the entry was verified? | none | `git log -1 --format=%at` on the entry, then on each `covers:` path | G-F14, G-F12, M15 |
+| Is this decision still true of the code? | none | read the entry and the code; the design gives this to the background subagent | SOURCE_OF_TRUTH:68–73 |
+| What should I load when resuming? | none | `INDEX.md` and the task's `state.md` | M17, M18, M19 |
+| A map of the whole repo | none | `PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md`, `INDEX.md` | M18, G-F18 |
+| A `covers:` path that is not Python | none | git and grep | G-F18 |
+| Is the graph I am about to query current? | none — do not check, rebuild whole | rebuild, or use git | G-F5, G-F18 |
+
+**Costs, and where there are none.** Measured on other corpora or on this machine, never on a real
+`project-memory/`: grep over 10,000 synthetic entries, 0.038 s for one word and 0.060 s for a structured chain
+(`S-F13`); grep plus opening every hit, about 226K tokens per question (`S-F4`, `S-F13` — opencode's figure, and D3
+records that the other two report no such measurement); an `INDEX.md` scan, under 0.01 s and minimal tokens
+(`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`); reading one entry, roughly 270 tokens by the 4-characters-per-token assumption (`S-F5`, not
+measured); a git last-change lookup at the tool's timing resolution (`G-F12`). **`GAP` for everything that matters
+most**: no FTS5-vs-grep measurement on short entries (`S-F5`), no vector measurement on a corpus shaped like
+`entries/` (`V-F11`), no graph-vs-grep head-to-head on this repo's own questions (`G-F10`, `M21`), and no wrapper
+exists to time a filtered graph query (`G-F18`). The last three are now measured on this repo's own code and
+docs. See **Measured here: development and testing** below.
+
+**Still unresolved.** Six disagreements the three engines did not settle, kept as positions and not averaged (D1–D6
+in the run record): whether FTS5 or the vector index comes first, or whether memory needs a store at all; whether the
+FTS/vector benefit has been measured at all; the true token cost of grep-plus-open; the cost of `INDEX.md`; whether
+`INDEX.md` carries `covers:` and status; and what counts as a *fired* trigger.
+
+**Why no SQLite is visible in this repo.** Three reasons, and none is a rejection. No `.db` is ever on disk — the
+patterns are gitignored and the indexes are rebuilt on demand. The SQLite this repo does have, `ancient_games/index.py`,
+indexes the **referee's journal**, not memory: it is the working precedent for this exact pattern (7 tables, derived,
+~8 ms per journal) but it answers a different question. And the memory cache is gated behind a trigger that has not
+fired, because `project-memory/` does not exist yet. The same is true of the code graph: `code_graph/code_graph.py`
+already exists, deterministic and full-rebuild, and is the option this document names if that trigger fires.
+
+A fourth reason belongs to the first draft. Its research asked only whether each cache is needed to search memory
+*entries*, so it measured nothing on the questions asked during development and testing. The next section measures
+those.

    12	question whose words are likely in the entry text, once `INDEX.md` can no longer list every active entry under its size
    13	limit and ranked results with snippets save opened files (`S-F1`, `S-F4`). **The vector index** is for the same
    14	discovery when the question's wording differs from the entry's, once the documented miss trigger has fired
    15	(`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`). **The code graph** is only for a multi-hop structural question about Python code, after its
    16	trigger, and only as a lower bound (`G-F8`, `G-F17`, `G-F18`, `M5`, `R5.3`). None of the three answers a structured
    17	header query (path, status, supersession), an exact identifier, an exhaustive list, proof of absence, history, or
    18	"what is true" — `INDEX.md`, grep, git and reading the entry do, and every cache result stays a candidate until its
    19	source is read (`M19`).
    20	
    21	**Thinly or off-brief.** Notes on answer quality, not on the rules.
    22	- **claude** gives the longest and richest cost evidence, but its closing section (its last 12 of 76 lines) is process
    23	  material rather than decision rules: a negotiation with another agent (`ancient-games-1f`) about splitting the work,
    24	  a request that that agent measure the filtered-graph and graph-vs-grep costs, and an explicit statement that every
    25	  command needing approval was refused, so it ran no measurement itself. Its rule ends by labelling itself *"My reading
    26	  of G-F7a, G-F14, G-F17, G-F18, M18, and not measured on this repo."* That disclosed contact with another agent about
    27	  the same question is a caveat on the three answers' independence: claude says it did not open the other engines'
    28	  node files, but it did coordinate with a fourth party on the same brief.
    29	- **opencode** is the thinnest of the three: seven table rows, a rule with no inline evidence id, no `M*` id anywhere,
    30	  and a three-bullet *Not found / Not checked*. It also folds `S-F4`'s vendor BM25 study into its cost table as if it
    31	  measured this project's FTS5/grep tokens; codex and claude both refuse exactly that transfer (§ Where the engines
    32	  disagree, D2/D3).
    33	- **codex** is on-brief and in the exact brief shape, and is the only engine to label part of its own answer
    34	  `model knowledge` (`M6`'s role recommendation) and to run a requirement-check on itself; its note that `0.00 s` is
    35	  the timer's resolution "not proof of zero cost" is the sharpest reading of the recorded numbers.
    36	
    37	## Decision table
    38	
    39	One row per question an agent actually asks. "use this store" names the store or `none`; `none` means a cache is not
    40	the answer and the `tool instead` column is the path.
    41	
    42	| the question | use this store | tool instead | why | engines agreeing | evidence |
    43	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    44	| What do we already know or decide about X? (free text; the question's words are likely in the entry text) | **SQLite FTS** — conditional: only when `INDEX.md` is over its size limit and ranking/snippets save opened files | `INDEX.md`, then grep `entries/`, then read the entry | FTS5 returns ranked results with snippets; grep returns an unranked list and every opened file costs context | opencode: yes (`S-F1`, `S-F4`). codex: baseline first, "alongside keyword search", never names FTS5 (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`). claude: no store for any memory question (`G-F2`, `G-F3`, `M17`, `M19`) — see D1 | S-F1, S-F4, V-F11, V-F13, M17, M19, G-F2, G-F3 |
    45	| What prior entry matches when my words differ from the entry's? (find by meaning) | **vector index** — conditional, after the documented miss trigger has fired | `INDEX.md`, grep, then read | Meaning-based discovery is the proposed addition; benefit on this entry format is unmeasured | codex: conditional yes (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`). opencode: FTS5 no for meaning, vector if built (`S-F3`). claude: silent on vector | V-F11, V-F13, M4, S-F3 |
    46	| Which entries cover the file I'm about to edit? | **none** | `INDEX.md`: match `covers:`, then read the entries | The question supplies an exact path; similarity adds an unnecessary discovery step | codex (`V-F21`, `M19`); claude (`G-F14`, `S-F13`); opencode (script/grep, `S-F13`) — claude hedges on whether `INDEX.md` carries `covers:`, see D5 | V-F21, M19, G-F14, S-F13 |
    47	| List **every** active decision covering path X (completeness) | **none** — a ranked top-k slice cannot establish completeness | exhaustive scan/filter of `INDEX.md` and entry headers; a Python script reading frontmatter | Script answers in 0.1 s at 10K entries, same hits as SQL, no index needed | codex (`V-F1`, `V-F23`); opencode (`S-F13`); claude (grep `covers:`, `G-F14`) | V-F1, V-F23, S-F13, G-F14 |
    48	| Where is `D-0012`, or the entry mentioning this exact error string? | **none** | filename lookup or literal grep, then read the entry | Embeddings can miss exact identifiers and technical terms | codex (`V-F7`, `V-F21`); opencode (explicit frontmatter links, grep, `S-F3`, `S-F13`) | V-F7, V-F21, S-F3, S-F13 |
    49	| Have we **never** recorded a restriction against this change? (proving absence) | **none** — no vector hit does not prove absence | exhaustive file/header search and reading | Negation also creates ranking failures | codex (`V-F20`, `V-F23`) | V-F20, V-F23 |
    50	| Which entries have status X? (structured header field) | **none** | `INDEX.md` scan, or a script over frontmatter | `INDEX.md` already carries status per line; links are explicit in frontmatter | opencode (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH:100`, `S-F3`, `S-F13`); codex (status / `superseded_by`, `V-F22`, `V-F23`) | SOURCE_OF_TRUTH:100, S-F3, S-F13, V-F22, V-F23 |
    51	| What supersedes Y, or what does `D-0012` depend on? | **none** | follow `supersedes` / `superseded_by` / `depends_on` links via grep | Supersession is frontmatter links, not edges in a graph and not a similarity rank | claude (`M13`, `M5`); codex (`V-F2`, `V-F22`, `V-F23`, `M13`); opencode (`S-F3`, `S-F13`) | M13, M5, V-F2, V-F22, V-F23, S-F3, S-F13 |
    52	| Why did we choose this design, or why is this line the way it is? | **none** (vector only to locate an unknown entry) | read the decision's **Why:** and its evidence; `git blame` / `git log -S` for code | A similar passage does not supply the complete rationale; git is the record | codex (`M19`, `s3-F21`); claude (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH`, `M19`); opencode (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH`) | M19, s3-F21, SOURCE_OF_TRUTH |
    53	| Did the covered code change after the entry was verified? (the drift check) | **none** | git: `git log -1 --format=%at -- <entry>`, then the same for each `covers:` path, and compare | `covers:` names paths and git is the record, so it cannot drift; a graph adds a drift class (4.3% of edges wrong, silently) | claude (`G-F12`, `G-F5`); codex (`M15`, `M16`); opencode (git, `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH`) | G-F12, G-F5, M15, M16 |
    54	| Is this decision or fact still true of the code? | **none** | read the entry and the code; the design gives this job to the background subagent (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH:68–73`) | "Does the entry still match" is a model-assigned job, not a model-free script's | claude (`code-graph §6`, `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH:68–73`); codex (read the source before acting, `V-F21`, `M19`) | SOURCE_OF_TRUTH:68–73, V-F21, M19 |
    55	| What should I load when resuming testing? | **none** | `INDEX.md` and the assigned task's `state.md` | Retrieval is on demand; caches are not startup context | codex (`M17`, `M18`, `M19`) | M17, M18, M19 |
    56	| What does changing F reach, through several hops, including files on no `covers:` list? | **code graph** — after the trigger, Python only; the answer is a lower bound | before the trigger, grep the import and call names and read | The graph's one lane, the hidden-dependency class; it is trigger condition 2 | claude (`G-F8`, `G-F17`, `G-F18`, `M5`, `R5.3`); codex and opencode did not address code structure | G-F8, G-F17, G-F18, M5, R5.3 |
    57	| Which tests reach function F, so I know what to run first? | **code graph** — conditional yes, after the trigger, as a lower bound | grep test names, then run the suite | Coverage is static reachability over call and fixture edges and the tool says it is a lower bound; use it to order tests, never to skip tests or claim "untested" *(claude's reading — the "never skip" half is labelled my reading by claude, not measured)* | claude (`code_graph.py` docstring, `G-F12`) | code_graph.py docstring, G-F12 |
    58	| Where is X defined, and exactly where is it called? | **none** | `grep -n`, then read the hit | The graph leaves exhaustive call-site grep and line-level code to the explorer; 3,874 unresolved calls against 2,000 call edges means "no callers" would be a silent partial answer | claude (`s3-F29`, `G-F12`) | s3-F29, G-F12 |
    59	| What does this function do? | **none** | read the file | The graph stores no line-level code | claude (`s3-F29`) | s3-F29 |
    60	| Is F unused or dead? | **none** | grep, then run the tests | 2,863 attribute, 927 dynamic and 84 name calls are unresolved, so a missing edge does not mean no caller | claude (`G-F12`) | G-F12 |
    61	| Give me a map of the whole repo | **none** — `M18` forbids loading a whole-graph dump, and this graph's dump is 2.6 MB | `PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md` and `INDEX.md` | Graph responses also leave more context resident (67k vs 18k tokens on VS Code, external tool) | claude (`M18`, `G-F12`, `G-F7b`, `s3-F24`) | M18, G-F12, G-F7b, s3-F24 |
    62	| A `covers:` path that isn't Python (docs, shell, JS) | **none** | git and grep | The in-repo tool reads `*.py` only, and silence on other paths looks like "nothing found" | claude (`G-F18`, `code-graph §5 option B`) | G-F18 |
    63	| A one-line comment changed in a large covered file: real change? | **none** | `git diff`, plus stdlib `ast` on that one file if precision is needed | Option E gets the precision from the diff without a repo-wide graph | claude (`code-graph §5 option E`) | code-graph §5 option E |
    64	| Is the graph I'm about to query current? | **none — don't check; rebuild whole** | rebuild, or use git | Drift cannot be cheaply detected, since detecting it costs the full rebuild; a cache is never patched | claude (`G-F5c`, `G-F5` drift, `R2`, `R3.1`) | G-F5c, G-F5, R2, R3.1 |
    65	
    66	## Cost
    67	
    68	One row per measured path, per source. Numbers from different sources are never averaged; where two engines give
    69	different figures for the same path they are two rows. `GAP` means no number exists — it is not a hole to fill.
    70	Every figure below is a recorded one; the three engines ran no first-party measurement on this project's memory, and
    71	one (claude) could run nothing at all.
    72	
    73	| path | time | tokens | source id |
    74	|---|---|---|---|
    75	| `INDEX.md` at query time — claude's reading | no extra read at query time; loaded at session start | GAP — no `INDEX.md` exists, and its size limit is EJ's number | M17, SOURCE_OF_TRUTH:126 |
    76	| `INDEX.md` scan — opencode's figure | <0.01 s (one line per active entry) | minimal — header only | SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md (opencode) |
    77	| `INDEX.md` → read selected entries — codex's figure | GAP | GAP — no measured index size or reading cost; avoiding an extra search is codex's reading, not a measured saving | M17, M19 |
    78	| grep over `entries/`, one-word query, 10,000 **synthetic** entries | 0.038 s | GAP — `S-F13` measured command time only (codex and claude both say tokens were not measured) | S-F13 |
    79	| grep + open every hit, 10,000 entries — opencode's figure | 0.038 s | ~226K per question (agent opens every hit) | S-F4, S-F13 |
    80	| structured grep chain ("active decision covering path X"), 10,000 synthetic entries | 0.060 s | GAP | S-F13 |
    81	| grep over `entries/`, 1,000 synthetic entries | 0.005 s one-word; 0.009 s structured chain | GAP | S-F13 |
    82	| grep rebuild | 0 s — grep reads the files directly | zero (no model call) | opencode, no id given |
    83	| read one entry | one file read | ~270 tokens per design-shaped entry (~1 KB at `S-F5`'s 4-characters-per-token assumption, which is not measured); real entries GAP | S-F13, S-F5 |
    84	| read a known entry and its evidence — codex's figure | GAP | GAP — `s3-F21`'s 382–980 tokens/question measures a separate rationale-synthesis system, not ordinary entry reading | s3-F21 |
    85	| git last-change lookup `git log -1 --format=%at -- <path>` | 0.00 s — the reported timing resolution, not proof of zero cost (codex) | GAP — one timestamp printed, no token count measured | G-F12 |
    86	| git directory history `git log --name-only -- ancient_games/` | 0.01 s, 40 distinct paths, 246 commits, 19 MB `.git` | GAP | G-F12 |
    87	| in-repo code graph, full rebuild | 0.94 s for 126 files; an earlier build 0.6 s for 110 files (15% more files for 57% more time does not show linear scaling) | output is 2,607,872 bytes of JSON ≈ 650,000 tokens derived at `S-F5`'s 4-characters/token, not a tokenizer run; forbidden by `M18` anyway | G-F12, G-F18, S-F5 |
    88	| in-repo code graph, filtered query | GAP — no wrapper exists | GAP — no wrapper exists | G-F18 |
    89	| graph vs grep, head-to-head on this repo's own questions | GAP | GAP | G-F10, M21 |
    90	| external indexer (codegraph), vendor benchmark, architecture question, 7 source-code repos, median of 4 runs | 53% faster | 62% fewer tokens, 44% cheaper; per repo Excalidraw 78% cheaper (2 vs 43 tool calls), Django 13% (3 vs 14), Gin about even (1 vs 7) | G-F7, G-F7a |
    91	| graph adoption, 258 trials on a FastAPI repo | 58% of trials with graph access made zero tool calls | GAP for tokens; hidden-dependency tasks 99.4% with graph vs 76.2% vanilla (accuracy, not tokens) | G-F8, G-F20 |
    92	| Blueprint run, structural graph vs none | GAP | ~63K tokens with a structural graph vs ~41K without | G-F9, s3-F32 |
    93	| vector search only (sqlite-vec), 1,000,000 × 128-dim vectors, k=20, author's Mac M1 | 33 ms (`vec0`); 17 ms (static) | GAP | V-F17 |
    94	| vector agent tokens, mem0 relevance tasks vs typed graph | GAP | ~40k vs ~35k (vendor paper) | V-F1, cited in s3-F34 |
    95	| vector/hybrid → read entry, end to end | GAP | GAP — the corpus and project-query comparison do not exist | V-F11 |
    96	| query embedding latency on this project | GAP | GAP | V-F18 |
    97	| FTS5 + snippet, 10,000 entries — opencode's figure | 0.0006 s | ~5.8K per question (ranked, snippet shows relevance) | S-F4, S-F13 |
    98	| FTS5 rebuild, 10,000 entries | 0.53 s | zero — no model call | S-F13 |
    99	| Python script, structured query, 10,000 entries | 0.104 s | same as SQL — returns the same hits | S-F13 |
   100	| SQL over a header table, structured query, 10,000 entries | 0.0008 s | same as the script | S-F13 |
   101	| FTS5 vs grep on a corpus of hundreds to a few thousand short memory entries | GAP | GAP | S-F5 |
   102	| journal index, `ancient_games/index.py` (in-repo precedent, not memory) | 8 ms per journal; derived and rebuildable | GAP | recon.md |
   103	| full test suite `env -u NO_COLOR python3 -m pytest -q` (433 tests) | GAP | GAP | G-F12 |
   104	| any path on a real `project-memory/` corpus | GAP | GAP | S-F5, V-F11 |
   105	
   106	## Triggers
   107	
   108	One line per store. Nothing is built before its own trigger fires; a trigger is observable; a number the sources do
   109	not give is left `OPEN: EJ's number`.
   110	
   111	- **SQLite FTS5** — fires when `INDEX.md` can no longer list every active entry under its size limit (**OPEN: EJ's
   112	  number** — `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH:126`, `CACHES.md` R5.1, `S-F5`) *and* the free-text question shares words with the
   113	  answer, so ranking and snippets beat unranked grep (`S-F1`, `S-F4`). opencode additionally records a grep crossover
   114	  of **~37K entries** (`S-F4`, `S-F5`, `S-F13`) — that is opencode's number, and it is a grep crossover, not the
   115	  `INDEX.md` size limit. Preconditions: `project-memory/` exists with active entries, and a fallback to grep must
   116	  exist when FTS5 is absent or stale (`S-F9`, `M6`).
   117	- **Vector index** — fires when the FTS trigger has already fired *and* recorded misses reach EJ's chosen count,
   118	  where each miss documents an existing entry that `INDEX.md`, grep and full-text search all missed and another route
   119	  found. **Both thresholds: `OPEN: EJ's number`**; corpus size alone does not justify vectors (`V-F11`, `V-F13`,
   120	  `M4`, `CACHES.md` R5). At query time the cache must match the current files and embedding model, search the active
   121	  set with status and `covers`, and return source filenames; a missing or mismatched cache/model means use the files
   122	  (`V-F22`, `V-F24`).
   123	- **Code graph** — fires on any **one** of three conditions (`R5.3`, `code-graph §6`): a `covers:` field names a
   124	  function or class instead of a path; the check must answer about a file on no `covers:` list; or path-level flags
   125	  raise too many false alarms (**rate `OPEN: EJ's number`**). It also needs a demonstrated failure of the baseline on
   126	  this project's own tasks (`M21` — none is recorded), a wrapper that returns only the answer and never the dump
   127	  (`M18` — none found), a whole rebuild just before answering and never a patch (`R2`, `R3.1`, `G-F5` drift), and an
   128	  answer that states its scope "Python only, lower bound" (`G-F18`, `G-F12`). The threshold for "grep took too many
   129	  tool calls" is **`OPEN: EJ's number`** — `G-F7a` shows the range (7 calls against 28–43) but no threshold for this
   130	  repo. How to word the instruction to call it is `model knowledge` (claude's label).
   131	
   132	## Where the engines disagree
   133	
   134	No average and no silent winner. `unresolved` means the three files cannot settle it.
   135	
   136	- **D1 — What answers a free-text memory question?** opencode: **FTS5 first**, when `INDEX.md` is over capacity and
   137	  ranking matters, with vector only for meaning (`S-F1`, `S-F4`, `S-F3`). codex: **baseline first** — `INDEX.md` and
   138	  grep — then vector "alongside keyword search" when wording mismatch defeats the baseline; codex names **no FTS5**
   139	  anywhere (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`). claude: **no store at all** for any memory question (`G-F2`, `G-F3`, `G-F10`,
   140	  `M17`, `M19`). **unresolved.**
   141	- **D2 — Is the FTS5/vector benefit measured?** opencode presents FTS5 against grep as a material token saving
   142	  (~5.8K vs ~226K per question; `S-F4`, `S-F13`). codex calls the vector benefit *"guidance, not a measured advantage
   143	  here"* (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`) and says its synthetic timings *"do not establish vector savings."* claude says
   144	  memory-corpus costs are GAP and there is no head-to-head (`S-F5`, `V-F11`, `G-F10`). **unresolved** — whether
   145	  `S-F4`'s 1,144-document BM25 study transfers to this project's 10,000-entry claim is not settled by the three files.
   146	- **D3 — Token cost of grep + opening hits.** opencode: **~226K per question** at 10,000 entries (`S-F4`, `S-F13`).
   147	  claude: **GAP per question**, "because it depends on hits and grep is unranked". codex: **tokens were not
   148	  measured** (`S-F13`). **unresolved.**
   149	- **D4 — Cost of `INDEX.md` at query time.** claude: **no extra read**, it is loaded at session start (`M17`).
   150	  opencode: **<0.01 s** scan, minimal header-only tokens (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`). codex: **time GAP and tokens GAP**
   151	  (`M17`, `M19`). Two engines read the same `M17` differently. **unresolved.**
   152	- **D5 — Does `INDEX.md` carry `covers:` (and status) per line?** codex asserts the `covers:` match directly
   153	  (`V-F21`). opencode asserts status per line (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH:100`). claude hedges: `RECOMMENDATION.md`'s Layout
   154	  says the lines carry `covers`, `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` says only *"one line per active entry"* — so if `covers` is not
   155	  there, that row falls back to grep over `entries/`. **unresolved** (a documentation conflict; the three files do
   156	  not settle which doc is right).
   157	- **D6 — What counts as a fired trigger?** opencode treats a **size crossover (~37K entries)** as a build condition
   158	  (`S-F4`, `S-F5`, `S-F13`). codex treats only a **recorded miss count** as one and says corpus size alone does not
   159	  justify vectors (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`). claude requires a **demonstrated baseline failure** (`M21`) plus a fired
   160	  R5.3 condition. **unresolved** — the three name three different currencies.
   161	- **Not disagreements, for the record (one engine only, so the table must not read as consensus):** the code graph's
   162	  lane, its triggers and its drift rule rest on claude alone (codex and opencode did not address code structure); the
   163	  `~37K` grep crossover, the `0.0006 s` FTS5 figure and the `~5.8K`/`~226K` token pair rest on opencode alone; the
   164	  vector timings rest on codex alone.
   165	
   166	## What this repo already has
   167	
   168	From `recon.md`, which answers EJ's *"why I don't see the sqlite"* — and the answer is three separate reasons, none
   169	of them that SQLite was rejected.
   170	
   171	- **`ancient_games/index.py`** (17.7 KB, 2026-09-08) — a SQLite index in Python, the minimal index built under
   172	  `docs/STORE_DESIGN_DECISION.md`: *"Build the minimal SQLite index. Defer the graph layer entirely."* Seven tables,
   173	  five run-scoped queries, differential-tested against the lints. It is **the referee's journal** — claims, checks,
   174	  returns — **not project memory**. It is evidence of the exact pattern `CACHES.md` proposes for memory (derived,
   175	  rebuildable, 8 ms per journal), proven here once, but for a different question.
   176	- **`code_graph/code_graph.py`** (38 KB, 2026-10-06) — this repo's own deterministic, full-rebuild, Python-only code
   177	  graph, for the code-graph tool. It is **the code**, **not memory**. `CACHES.md` already names it as the code-graph
   178	  option if that trigger fires; it is evidence the derived-cache pattern is proven here a second time.
   179	- **No `.db` file is ever on disk to see** — `.gitignore` carries `*.db`, `*.sqlite`, `*.sqlite3`; the index is built
   180	  on demand and never committed, so a `find` for those extensions returns nothing.
   181	- **No `project-memory/`** — the markdown memory tree the whole design describes does not exist yet, so the memory
   182	  cache is gated behind a trigger that has not fired. SQLite is not missing; it is *not yet justified*.
   183	
   184	## Not found / Not checked
   185	
   186	Merged from the three engines' own closing sections.
   187	
   188	- **No first-party measurement.** claude ran nothing: every command needing approval was refused in its session, so
   189	  it could not rebuild the graph, time git or grep, or run a filter. codex and opencode did not rerun the sibling
   190	  researchers' synthetic timings either; those were the researchers' own checks, not independent validation.
   191	- **No memory-corpus number anywhere.** Time and tokens for every path on a real `project-memory/` are GAP
   192	  (`S-F5`, `V-F11`) because that corpus does not exist. `INDEX.md` does not exist either, so its read cost and its
   193	  size limit are GAP (`SOURCE_OF_TRUTH:126`).
   194	- **No measured crossover or benefit.** No measured vector benefit or time/token crossover on typed project-memory
   195	  entries (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `V-F18`); no FTS5-vs-grep measurement on a corpus of hundreds to a few thousand short
   196	  memory entries (`S-F5`); no graph-vs-grep head-to-head on this repo's own questions (`G-F10`, `M21`); no
   197	  measurement of the token cost of unranked grep output against an FTS5 snippet except the BM25 study (`S-F4`).
   198	- **Not checked.** A project query replay, cache freshness, or retrieval quality here (`V-F11`); raw page text of
   199	  codex's cited sources — all its reads went through extracts; Windows/macOS SQLite FTS5 availability; actual agent
   200	  token usage on this project's memory shape (opencode). claude read only `s3-F4`, `F24`, `F25`, `F29`, `F31` of the
   201	  findings file and took `s3-F32` second-hand through `G-F9`; it did not search the web, and its external `G-F*`
   202	  quotes are second-hand through the research node. codex checked only the primary `V-F17` benchmark online and did
   203	  not independently reverify the other external findings.
   204	- **Still unassessed.** Resolution quality: `G-F12` lists it as unassessed, so how many of the 3,874 unresolved calls
   205	  are real in-repo calls is unknown. Full test-suite wall time (433 tests) is GAP, as is any saving from
   206	  graph-ordered tests. Git cost at larger histories is GAP — only 246 commits were measured. The filtered graph
   207	  query's cost is GAP because no wrapper exists (`G-F18`).
   208	- **Open questions left to EJ, not filled in.** The `INDEX.md` size limit; the vector miss count; the code graph's

    24	## By category
    25	
    26	`file hit@5` counts a gold `path::name` as hit when the route returns its file. `symbol hit@5` needs the symbol itself: an entry naming it, the top-level def/class holding it or a method of it, a module-code chunk for a module-level name, and for `rg` a printed match line inside the symbol's lines. A gold given as a plain file scores the same on both. **The best route is chosen on symbol hit@5.**
    27	
    28	`chars` is the mean number of characters of output read up to and including the first line where the route names a gold file as its answer (`# <path> (`, or a line starting `<path>:`), over the answers that named one; `n` is how many did. A miss is left out, never scored 0. What each route prints differs -- rg: a `# path (N matching lines)` header per file plus at most 40 match lines of up to 300 characters; fts: one 24-token snippet per path; vec: the first 300 characters per path; hybrid: the vec or fts block per path; graph: one id per line -- so `chars` measures the print format as much as the store.
    29

    35	| fts | 0.625 | 0.75 | 355.7143 (7) | 1477.25 | 0.0009 |
    36	| vec | 0.0 | 0.5 | 837.0 (5) | 2997.0 | 0.0018 |
    37	| hybrid | 0.5 | 0.75 | 91.3333 (6) | 2804.625 | 0.0025 |

13:slice), s3-F20/F34 (MOOSEDev: structured question types only), s3-F25/F27 (silent drift of a live index), from
19:**Question:** "What default FTS5 tokenizer does the `hollowfern` markdown indexer use, according to its docs?"
24:- Two web searches: `hollowfern markdown indexer FTS5 tokenizer` and `"hollowfern" github markdown index`; neither returned any project of that name. The bare search `hollowfern` returned only holly-fern plant pages.
31:What I can say that is true, and is not an answer about hollowfern: **SQLite's own FTS5 default tokenizer is `unicode61`** (F2). Any tool that creates an FTS5 table without a `tokenize=` option gets it. That says nothing about a tool I could not find.
37:**F1 — CONFIRMED (stated rationale, not a measurement).** The `monorepo-agents-harness` maintainers list what grep cannot do for their knowledge folder, which is the closest thing found to an argued case for FTS5 over grep for agent memory.
40:Limit: it is a feature proposal. It reports no accuracy or token number for FTS5 against grep.
42:**F2 — CONFIRMED.** FTS5's documented behaviour that matters for this project.
44:Quotes: `"The unicode61 tokenizer, based on the Unicode 6.1 standard. This is the default."`; `"The better the match, the numerically smaller the value returned."` (bm25); `"The contents of columns qualified with the UNINDEXED column option are not added to the FTS index."`
45:So header fields such as `kind`, `status`, `covers` can ride along as `UNINDEXED` columns or sit in a plain table next to the FTS table.
47:**F3 — INFERRED from F1, F2 and my measurement F13.** Which questions an agent asks, split by what the store adds over `grep` plus `INDEX.md`:
49:- *Header-field queries* ("every active decision whose `covers` includes path X", "what supersedes Y"): a SQL `WHERE` over a table of header fields answers these. grep over `entries/` answers them with a pipeline of greps or a short script (F13 shows both returning the same hits). No source found measures an agent doing either.
50:- *Backlinks, orphans, broken `supersedes` links*: needs a link table or a script; FTS5 itself does not provide it (the harness in F5 builds a separate `links` table).
55:**F4 — CONFIRMED.** The one controlled scaling measurement found for BM25 against an agent using grep over files. The "File-System Agent" had `list_dir`, `grep` (fixed string, up to 30 matching paths) and `read_doc` (first 8,000 characters), 80 LLM calls per question; the benchmark is a general RAG corpus, not code or memory entries.
57:Quotes: `"Around 10 million corpus tokens, BM25 overtakes it and leads at every larger shared tier"`; `"its sequential exploration costs 39 times more query tokens at the bedrock."` Numbers from the extract of the HTML: smallest tier 1,144 documents and 1.7M corpus tokens; tokens per question at that tier BM25 5.8K against File-System Agent 226K; at full corpus 5.8K against 895K (agent figure includes retries); BM25 leads "with a margin near 20 points at full scale." Also: the agent leads "at the smallest shared tiers."
60:**F5 — INFERRED from F4 and F13.** Where this project sits against that crossover. The design says an entry is "the claim in one or two sentences, then Why:, then How to apply:", so about 1 KB each. My synthetic entries of that shape came to 1.06 MB per 1,000 entries (F13). At an assumed 4 characters per token (my assumption, not measured) that is about 0.27M tokens per 1,000 entries, so the 10M-token crossover in F4 would be near 37,000 entries, and even the 1.7M-token smallest tier in F4 would be near 6,000 entries. `project-memory/` does not exist yet in this repo (checked), so the real corpus size is unknown. The only count available: `docs/` and `runs/` hold 364 markdown files today. **No measurement exists of when FTS5 or BM25 beats grep for an agent on a corpus of a few hundred to a few thousand short entries.** That is a GAP, not a finding.
62:**F6 — CONFIRMED, and it cuts the other way.** Grep matched or beat vector retrieval in a controlled agent study.
63:URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15184 (submitted 2026-05-14), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2605.15184v1.
65:Limits: LongMemEval conversational QA (116 questions), not code memory. It compares grep with vector, not with BM25. The extract reported no token or cost figures. Relevant here mainly to the vector cache; for the FTS store it shows grep is a strong baseline.
67:**F7 — CONFIRMED (vendor's own small benchmark).** A grep baseline recalled almost everything but was less precise than a hybrid index.
72:**F8 — UNPROVEN.** Claude Code's own default is grep-style search, not an index (commonly reported). I did not open a primary source for it, so it is not used above.
76:All of these are 2026; none is measured on a corpus like ours. Rebuild time and size are listed only where a source gave them; the local numbers are in F13.
78:**F9 — CONFIRMED. `monorepo-agents-harness` `kb-index.sh`.** Closest match to the brief: full rebuild, gitignored, grep fallback.
80:Quotes: `"Rebuild in full every time."`; `"`query` compares the index build time with the newest source mtime and rebuilds first if the index is stale."`; `"A stale index is worse than no index."`; if `sqlite3` is missing or lacks FTS5 it should `"print one warning and fall back to the current grep path."`; rebuild `"finishes in under a second"` at about 1.4k files. PR: `"a deterministic, self-healing SQLite FTS5 index over `knowledge/**/*.md`"`, built into a temp file then renamed over `knowledge/.index.sqlite`; derived columns `kind`, `workspace`, `task_slug`, `date` come from page shapes, not frontmatter. Tests: 38/38; removing self-heal caused 2 failures and removing atomic publish caused 4.
83:**F10 — CONFIRMED. Scott Spence's `node:sqlite` + FTS5 markdown tool** (and its "wiki0" successor).
85:Quotes: `"One chunk per heading, so results point at a section, not a whole file."`; the index command starts with `db.exec('DELETE FROM chunks')` and reinserts everything; `"with a SQLite index that can be rebuilt any time for search, backlinks and facts."`; facts are `"decisions, requirements, risks and assumptions so they could be queried on their own."` Numbers: about 260 posts as 2,647 chunks in under half a second including Node startup; search about 50 ms; in a real project docs grew from 65 to 291 and agents called the CLI 1,122 times across 327 sessions. FTS5 table made with no tokenizer option (default). Staleness: not discussed; full rebuild each run. Index size: not given. No comparison with grep is made in the post.
87:**F11 — CONFIRMED. memweave** (Python; hybrid BM25 + optional vectors).
91:**F12 — CONFIRMED (partly). Other tools, one line each.**
98:**F13 — MEASURED HERE (my own script; not independent evidence).** A synthetic corpus (random words, entries shaped like the design: frontmatter with `id`, `kind`, `status`, `covers`, `superseded_by`, then about 1 KB body), one run each, on this machine (Python 3.12, SQLite 3.45.1, WSL2). Script: `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-smoke01-dev-ancient-games/343bf18f-e2d9-4dcf-8db5-0ed3524f59db/scratchpad/bench/bench.py`. Full rebuild = read every file, parse frontmatter, insert into one FTS5 table plus one header table.
104:| 10,000 | 10.6 MB | 21.9 MB | 0.53 s | 0.104 / 0.060 / 0.0008 s | 0.038 / 0.0006 s |
107:What it does and does not show. At every size the grep route answers in well under half a second, so **latency is not a reason for a cache at this scale**; the index is about twice the markdown size. The 50,000-entry grep timing printed a negative number (the WSL clock moved), so I discard it. For the first three rows the structured query matched zero entries (the random path did not occur), so those timings are a full scan with no hits. The python script and the SQL returned the same count only where hits existed (4 and 4 at 50,000). Token cost to the agent is the real axis and this test does not measure it: it does not run an agent. The gain F4 reports is on tokens and accuracy, not wall-clock.
111:**F14 — CONFIRMED.** SQLite itself warns about an FTS5 index drifting from its source, and gives a repair.
113:Limit, INFERRED: `integrity-check` compares the index with the table it indexes. It does not compare that table with the markdown files on disk. I found no SQLite feature that does, so file drift must be caught by the tool (F15). Not tested here.
115:**F15 — CONFIRMED (the mechanisms in use).** How the tools above detect or prevent drift from the files:
116:- mtime comparison on every query, rebuild first if stale, atomic temp-file-then-rename publish (F9, quotes above).
117:- Full delete-and-reinsert on every index run, so drift lasts only until the next run (F10).
118:- SHA-256 per file and chunk, unchanged ones skipped (F11; and sqlite-memory in F12).
119:- A grep fallback if the index or FTS5 is missing (F9). This is the same property as M6, "its absence must not prevent reading memory."
120:Not found: any of these tools reporting a measured stale-answer rate. The only measured silent index drift in the record is the code graph, not FTS (s3-F25, s3-F27). The failure class is the same (a derived index that is not rebuilt when files change). Nothing shows FTS5 over markdown is prone to it.
122:**F16 — MEASURED HERE (small test, `python3` stdlib `sqlite3`, SQLite 3.45.1).** Two query-time traps with the default tokenizer, on a row containing `D-0012 covers src/auth/token_store.py and the retry_budget setting`:
124:- With the tokenizer changed to `unicode61 tokenchars '-_/.'` (a plausible attempt to keep ids and paths whole), the queries `token_store`, `retry`, `budget` and `auth` returned **0 rows, silently**; the quoted full path still matched. `trigram` matched every query tried. The default matched `token_store`, `retry`, `budget`, `auth` and `"auth/token"`.
125:So a wrong tokenizer choice yields a wrong zero rather than an error. I tried only these 8 queries and 4 tokenizers on one row.
127:**F17 — INFERRED from F1 and F10.** Result granularity. Chunking by heading (F10) returns a section; one-file-per-entry (the design) could index one row per file. Which is right is not settled by any source found.
129:**F18 — INFERRED from s3-F20/F34 and F6.** Where the evidence says a richer store helps and where it does not: MOOSEDev's advantage is on supersession and set questions, with relevance recall and token cost "largely equivalent" (s3-F34); a lexical store is not shown to win on relevance either. So for this project the case, if any, rests on the header-field questions in F3, not on search quality.
135:- **Option A — no cache.** `INDEX.md` plus `rg` over `entries/`, and a short stdlib Python script that reads frontmatter for the structured questions (F13 shows 0.1 s at 10,000 entries). Cost: no ranking, no snippets.
136:- **Option B — smallest FTS cache.** One stdlib `sqlite3` script: full rebuild into a temp file, `os.replace` over `project-memory/.cache/index.sqlite`; one FTS5 table (default tokenizer, one row per entry file, header fields as `UNINDEXED` columns) and optionally a header-fields table; rebuild when the newest file mtime is newer than the index, as in F9; fall back to `rg` if the file or FTS5 is missing. Measured rebuild 0.53 s and 22 MB at 10,000 synthetic entries (F13); the design's M6 holds because absence changes nothing for readers.
137:- **Option C — B plus link table.** Adds `depends_on` / `supersedes` / `superseded_by` rows for backlink, orphan and broken-link queries (as the harness `links` command in F9). This is the structured-query use; the drift-check script named in the design as "not decided" could read it, but that is a design matter, not found here.
138:- **Choices a design must still make** (listed, not decided): tokenizer (F16), row grain (F17), staleness test (mtime as in F9, or hash as in F11), who triggers a rebuild, whether query and rebuild share one command (F9 does).
142:- Any docs, repo or package for a `hollowfern` markdown indexer, and therefore its default tokenizer (planted question: GAP).
143:- Any measurement of FTS5 or BM25 against grep for an agent working on a small (hundreds to a few thousand) corpus of short memory entries, on accuracy or tokens.
146:- Index size and rebuild time in the published sources, apart from F9 ("under a second" at about 1.4k files) and F10 (under half a second at 2,647 chunks); no source gave an on-disk size.
147:- A primary source for the claim that Claude Code's default search is grep without an index (F8).
155:- Actual `grep` or `rg` token cost to an agent (output size returned into context) against an FTS5 `snippet()` result; not measured.

     1	# Proposed changes to the caches vision, for EJ
     2	
     3	The vision is `docs/memory-system/vision-statements/caches-memory-vision-statements.md`. It is EJ's, so it has not been
     4	edited. Below are three proposals that come from the measurements in `bench/results.md` (ids `BM-*` in `CACHES.md`).
     5	Each says what the vision says now, what the measurements show, and the wording that would follow. Accept, change or
     6	reject each one.
     7	
     8	## P1: who it is for
     9	
    10	- **Now:** "An agent retrieving an entry on demand."
    11	- **Measured:** the questions that cost tokens during development and testing are about code and docs: where X is
    12	  defined, what calls X, which tests to run, why we decided X. On those, full-text search and the graph print far
    13	  less before naming a right file (BM-1, BM-3, BM-4, BM-6; a proxy for tokens, not a token count).
    14	- **Proposed:** "An agent developing or testing this project, retrieving memory entries, code or docs on demand."
    15	
    16	## P2: "None of them is needed today"
    17	
    18	- **Now:** "None of them is needed today."
    19	- **Measured:** for memory entries this still holds: `project-memory/` does not exist. For code and docs (490 files
    20	  here), full-text prints about a sixth of grep's characters before naming a right file (BM-6; a proxy, not tokens), and the graph names
    21	  callers and test reach in tens of characters (BM-1, BM-3, BM-7).
    22	- **Proposed:** "For memory entries, none is needed until its trigger fires. For code and docs, full-text and the
    23	  code graph are worth having now. The vector index is not, on this evidence (BM-1, BM-7)."
    24	
    25	## P3: what an empty answer means
    26	
    27	- **Now:** not stated.
    28	- **Measured:** the graph returned nothing for tests that run the code as a separate process (BM-8). Read as "no
    29	  tests", that would skip tests that exist.
    30	- **Proposed**, as a new never-rule: "It never treats an empty answer as proof of absence: an empty answer means *not
    31	  seen*, and the agent falls back to the files."
    32	
    33	## What stays as written
    34	
    35	The never-rules, unchanged:
    36	- never the source of truth;
    37	- never patched;
    38	- never read at session start;
    39	- never a second thing to keep in sync.

     1	# Proposed changes to the caches vision, for EJ
     2	
     3	The vision is `docs/memory-system/vision-statements/caches-memory-vision-statements.md`. It is EJ's, so it has not been
     4	edited. Below are three proposals that come from the measurements in `bench/results.md` (ids `BM-*` in `CACHES.md`).
     5	Each says what the vision says now, what the measurements show, and the wording that would follow. Accept, change or
     6	reject each one.
     7	
     8	## P1: who it is for
     9	
    10	- **Now:** "An agent retrieving an entry on demand."
    11	- **Measured:** the questions that cost tokens during development and testing are about code and docs: where X is
    12	  defined, what calls X, which tests to run, why we decided X. On those, full-text search and the graph print far
    13	  less before naming a right file (BM-1, BM-3, BM-4, BM-6; a proxy for tokens, not a token count).
    14	- **Proposed:** "An agent developing or testing this project, retrieving memory entries, code or docs on demand."
    15	
    16	## P2: "None of them is needed today"
    17	
    18	- **Now:** "None of them is needed today."
    19	- **Measured:** for memory entries this still holds: `project-memory/` does not exist. For code and docs (490 files
    20	  here), full-text prints about a sixth of grep's characters before naming a right file (BM-6; a proxy, not tokens), and the graph names
    21	  callers and test reach in tens of characters (BM-1, BM-3, BM-7).
    22	- **Proposed:** "For memory entries, none is needed until its trigger fires. For code and docs, full-text and the
    23	  code graph are worth having now. The vector index is not, on this evidence (BM-1, BM-7)."
    24	
    25	## P3: what an empty answer means
    26	
    27	- **Now:** not stated.
    28	- **Measured:** the graph returned nothing for tests that run the code as a separate process (BM-8). Read as "no
    29	  tests", that would skip tests that exist.
    30	- **Proposed**, as a new never-rule: "It never treats an empty answer as proof of absence: an empty answer means *not
    31	  seen*, and the agent falls back to the files."
    32	
    33	## What stays as written
    34	
    35	The never-rules, unchanged:
    36	- never the source of truth;
    37	- never patched;
    38	- never read at session start;
    39	- never a second thing to keep in sync.
    40	
    41	The "How we know it works" test is also unchanged; this run's determinism check is that test (0 differences in 180).
    42	
    43	## Limits on this evidence
    44	
    45	- The numbers come from one repo and 40 questions, with n = 6 to 10 per category.
    46	- Token cost is measured as characters read, a proxy, not as tokens.
    47	- The harness repair was not re-audited.

    34	
    35	**Caveat on that catch, recorded:** the verifier says it confirmed the alteration *"by diffing the planted file
    36	against the clean `DECISION-RULES.md`"*. Both files sit in the same run folder, so the plant was partly given away
    37	by the layout rather than found only by checking the sources. Its item-1 evidence does cite the source line, but the
    38	catch is weaker than a blind source-only check would have been. Next time the planted copy goes in a folder that
    39	does not also hold the clean one.

    44	  another agent (`ancient-games-1f`) about splitting the work, in a parallel run

    46	  this repo. claude states it did not open the other engines' node files, but it did coordinate with a fourth party
    47	  on the same question.
    48	- **opencode was the thinnest answer** — 7 table rows, no `M*` id, and it folds `S-F4`'s vendor BM25 study into this

    50	- **Every cost for a real `project-memory/` is `GAP`.** No first-party measurement exists: no FTS5-vs-grep on short
    51	  entries (`S-F5`), no vector measurement on a corpus shaped like `entries/` (`V-F11`), no graph-vs-grep head-to-head
    52	  (`G-F10`, `M21`).
