     1	### The rule
     2	Use the vector index on demand, alongside keyword search, to discover a relevant entry when the question’s wording differs from the entry’s and the documented miss trigger has fired; this is guidance, not a measured advantage here (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`). Do not use it for exact lookups, exhaustive lists, proving absence, history, or deciding what is true: use the index, files, links, or git, and read the source before acting (`V-F21`, `V-F23`, `M19`).
     3	
     4	### Decision table
     5	| the question someone asks | use the vector index? | the tool that answers instead | why | evidence |
     6	|---|---|---|---|---|
     7	| “What memory applies to the file I’m about to edit?” | No | `INDEX.md`: match `covers:`, then read the entries | The question supplies an exact path; similarity adds an unnecessary discovery step. | My reading: `V-F21`, `M19` |
     8	| “Where is `D-0012`, or the entry mentioning this error string?” | No | Filename lookup or literal grep, then read the entry | Embeddings can miss exact identifiers and technical terms. | `V-F7`, `V-F21` |
     9	| “What prior lesson explains a tool quietly overlooking newly added code?” | Conditional: when wording mismatch defeats the baseline | First `INDEX.md` and grep; after the trigger, hybrid retrieval finds candidates, then read them | Meaning-based discovery is the proposed addition; benefit on this entry format is unmeasured. | `V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4` |
    10	| “List **every** active decision covering this path.” | No | Exhaustively inspect/filter `INDEX.md` and entry headers | A ranked top-k slice cannot establish completeness. | `V-F1`, `V-F23`; routing is my reading |
    11	| “Have we **never** recorded a restriction against this change?” | No | Exhaustive file/header search and reading | No vector hit does not prove absence; negation also creates ranking failures. | `V-F20`, `V-F23` |
    12	| “Why did we choose this design?” | Only to locate an unknown entry | Read the decision’s **Why:** and evidence; use git for code history | A similar passage does not supply the complete rationale. | `M19`, `s3-F21` |
    13	| “What replaced this decision, and which version governs?” | No | Read `status`, `superseded_by`, and the linked entries | Follow explicit lifecycle links rather than treating rank as currentness. | `V-F2`, `V-F22`, `V-F23`, `M13` |
    14	| “Did the covered code change after this entry was verified?” | No | Git diff/log against the recorded revision | This is a revision comparison, not a similarity question. | `M15`, `M16`; tool routing is my reading |
    15	| “What should I load when resuming testing?” | No | `INDEX.md` and the assigned task’s `state.md` | Retrieval is on demand; caches are not startup context. | `M17`, `M18`, `M19` |
    16	
    17	### Cost, in time and tokens
    18	All **end-to-end costs on real project memory are GAP**: the corpus and project-query comparison do not exist (`V-F11`). Available measurements provide orientation only:
    19	
    20	- **Vector/hybrid → read entry:** query embedding, fusion, source reading, and total query time/tokens: **GAP**. `V-F17` reports vector-search-only timings of **33 ms** (`vec0`) and **17 ms** (static), for **1,000,000 × 128-dimensional vectors, k=20**, on the author’s Mac M1—not this project or an embedded natural-language query. [Author’s benchmark](https://alexgarcia.xyz/blog/2024/sqlite-vec-stable-release/index.html).
    21	- **Vector agent tokens:** `V-F1` reports roughly **40k agent tokens** for mem0 relevance tasks, versus roughly **35k** for the typed graph. This vendor-paper comparison does not measure vectors versus this project’s grep or unchanged markdown (`s3-F34`).
    22	- **`INDEX.md` → read selected entries:** time **GAP**; tokens **GAP**. No measured index size or reading cost. Avoiding an additional search is my reading, not measured savings (`M17`, `M19`).
    23	- **Grep → read matching entries:** real-memory time/tokens **GAP**. The sibling researcher’s synthetic test measured **0.038 s** for one-word grep at **10,000 entries**, and **0.060 s** for a structured grep chain; tokens were not measured (`S-F13`, cited in `CACHES.md`). The structured query returned no hits at that size; these are command timings, not completed agent answers.
    24	- **Git → inspect change/history:** tokens and completed-answer time **GAP**. The sibling researcher reported **0.00 s** for a last-change lookup and **0.01 s** for a directory history scan here (`G-F12`, cited in `CACHES.md`); **0.00 s is the reported timing resolution**, not proof of zero cost.
    25	- **Read a known entry and its evidence:** time **GAP**; tokens **GAP**. The **382–980 tokens/question** in `s3-F21` measure a separate rationale-synthesis system, not ordinary entry reading.
    26	
    27	The synthetic timings were researchers’ own checks, not independent validation; I did not rerun them. They do not establish vector savings.
    28	
    29	### What must be true first
    30	The draft trigger is: the full-text trigger has fired because `INDEX.md` cannot list every active entry under EJ’s size limit; then recorded misses reach EJ’s chosen count. Each miss documents an existing entry that `INDEX.md`, grep, and full-text search missed and another route found. **Both thresholds remain GAP**; corpus size alone does not justify vectors (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `M4`; `CACHES.md` R5).
    31	
    32	At query time, the cache must match the current files and embedding model, search the active set with status and `covers`, and return source filenames. Missing or mismatched cache/model means use the files; stale vectors cannot answer. Results remain candidates until their entries and relevant evidence are read (`V-F22`, `V-F24`, `M6`—its role recommendation is labelled **model knowledge**—and `M19`).
    33	
    34	### Not found / Not checked
    35	- **Not found:** a measured time/token crossover or vector benefit on typed project-memory entries; query embedding latency here; EJ’s trigger thresholds (`V-F11`, `V-F13`, `V-F18`).
    36	- **Not checked:** a project query replay, cache freshness, or retrieval quality here. Recon reports no `project-memory/`; the existing SQLite indexes referee journals and the existing graph indexes Python code, neither this memory corpus.
    37	- **Evidence limit:** I read the specified local documents and checked the primary `V-F17` benchmark online; other external findings were not independently reverified.
    38	- **Requirement check, my check:** source, one thing, level, basis, and links pass; the trigger’s unit, weak-words, and can-it-fail checks remain incomplete until EJ supplies the thresholds. No values were invented.
     1	### The rule
     2	Use SQLite full-text (FTS5) when **ranking and snippets** materially reduce the tokens an agent spends opening unranked grep matches — typically when INDEX.md can no longer list every active entry and the free-text question shares words with the answer. Do not use it for **structured header-field queries** (path, status, supersession) or when grep already answers in under 0.1s.
     3	
     4	### Decision table
     5	
     6	| the question someone asks | use SQLite full-text (FTS5)? | the tool that answers instead | why | evidence |
     7	|---|---|---|---|---|
     8	| "which entries talk about X" (free-text, words likely in the text) | **Yes** — if INDEX.md is over capacity and ranking matters | grep + open files | FTS5 returns ranked results with snippets; grep returns unranked list, every opened file costs context | S-F1, S-F4 (BM25 used 5.8K tokens vs 226K for grep agent at 1,144 docs) |
     9	| "every active decision covering path X" | **No** | Python script reading frontmatter | Script answers in 0.1s at 10K entries; same result as SQL, no index needed | S-F13 (0.104s script vs 0.0008s SQL, but same hits) |
    10	| "what supersedes Y" | **No** | grep or script following `supersedes` links | Links are explicit in frontmatter; grep follows them directly | S-F3, S-F13 |
    11	| "find by meaning when words differ" | **No** | vector index (if built) | FTS5 matches words, not meaning | S-F3, CACHES.md vector section |
    12	| "which entries have status X" | **No** | INDEX.md scan | INDEX.md already carries status per line | SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md line 100 |
    13	| "why was this line changed" | **No** | git blame / git log | Git is the record, cannot drift | SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md |
    14	| small corpus (hundreds–few thousand entries) | **No** | grep | At 10K entries grep is 0.038-0.06s; crossover ~37K entries | S-F4, S-F5, S-F13 |
    15	
    16	### Cost, in time and tokens
    17	
    18	| path | time (measured) | tokens to agent | evidence |
    19	|---|---|---|---|
    20	| **grep + open files** (10K entries, one-word query) | 0.038s | ~226K per question (agent opens every hit) | S-F4, S-F13 |
    21	| **FTS5 + snippet** (10K entries) | 0.0006s | ~5.8K per question (ranked, snippet shows relevance) | S-F4, S-F13 |
    22	| **Python script** (structured query, 10K) | 0.104s | same as SQL (returns same hits) | S-F13 |
    23	| **SQL over header table** (structured, 10K) | 0.0008s | same as script | S-F13 |
    24	| **INDEX.md scan** | <0.01s (one line per active entry) | minimal — header only | SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md |
    25	| **FTS5 rebuild** (10K entries) | 0.53s | zero (no model call) | S-F13 |
    26	| **grep rebuild** | 0s (grep reads directly) | zero | — |
    27	
    28	### What must be true first
    29	
    30	The trigger that makes FTS5 worth building: **INDEX.md can no longer list every active entry under its size limit**. At that point the one-line scan of everything is gone, and ranked search with snippets is what replaces it (CACHES.md R5.1, S-F5).
    31

     1	### The rule
     2	Use the code graph only for a multi-hop structural question about Python code, such as what an edit reaches through several calls or imports, or which tests reach a function. The conditions are that the question lies on no `covers:` list, grep would need many tool calls, the trigger has fired, and a wrapper returns only the answer. Do not use it for any memory lookup, for anything `INDEX.md`, grep, git or reading one entry answers in a single step, for exact call sites or "no callers" claims, or for non-Python paths. *(My reading of G-F7a, G-F14, G-F17, G-F18, M18, and not measured on this repo.)*
     3	
     4	### Decision table
     5	Every memory row has a non-cache answer, so memory stays readable with no cache (M6). Rows 1–6 are memory questions; rows 7–15 are code and test questions.
     6	
     7	| the question someone asks | use the code graph? | the tool that answers instead | why | evidence |
     8	|---|---|---|---|---|
     9	| 1. What do we already know or decide about X? | No | `INDEX.md`, already in context from session start. If no line matches, grep `entries/`, then read the entry. | The graph indexes code by extension and Markdown is not in the list. No graph has been measured on a memory corpus. | G-F2, G-F3, G-F10, M17, M19 |
    10	| 2. Which entries cover the file I'm about to edit? | No | grep `covers:` in `INDEX.md` if its lines carry it, otherwise over `entries/`. | `covers:` is already a hand-kept entry→path index. | G-F14, S-F13 |
    11	| 3. Did the code an entry covers change after the entry? (the line-75 drift check) | No | git: `git log -1 --format=%at -- <entry>`, then the same for each `covers:` path, and compare. | `covers:` names paths, and git is the record, so it cannot drift. A graph adds a drift class (4.3% of edges wrong, silently). | G-F12, G-F5 drift, code-graph §6 |
    12	| 4. Is this decision or fact still true of the code? | No | Read the entry and the code. The design gives this job to the background subagent (SOURCE_OF_TRUTH lines 68–73). | "Does the entry still match" is a model-assigned job, not a model-free script's. | code-graph §6, SOURCE_OF_TRUTH:68–73 |
    13	| 5. Why is this line the way it is? | No | `git blame` or `git log -S`. For a decision, read the decision entry. | The parent design names these as the on-demand reads. | SOURCE_OF_TRUTH ("When it will be used"), M19 |
    14	| 6. What supersedes Y, or what does D-0012 depend on? | No | The entry header links (`supersedes`, `superseded_by`, `depends_on`), via grep. | Supersession is frontmatter links, not edges in a graph. | M13, M5 |
    15	| 7. Where is X defined, and exactly where is it called? | No | `grep -n`, then Read the hit. | The graph leaves "exhaustive call-site grep" and line-level code to the explorer (s3-F29). This repo has 3,874 unresolved calls against 2,000 call edges, so "no callers" would be a silent partial answer. | s3-F29, G-F12 |
    16	| 8. What does this function do? | No | Read the file. | The graph stores no line-level code (s3-F29). | s3-F29 |
    17	| 9. What does changing F reach, through several hops, including files on no `covers:` list? | Yes, after the trigger, Python only. The answer is a lower bound. | Before the trigger, grep the import and call names and Read. | This is the graph's one lane, the hidden-dependency class. It is trigger condition 2. | G-F8, G-F17, G-F18, M5, R5.3 |
    18	| 10. Which tests reach function F, so I know what to run first? | Conditional yes, after the trigger, as a lower bound | grep test names, then run the suite. | The tool computes coverage by static reachability over call and fixture edges, and says it is a lower bound. Use it to order tests, never to skip tests or to claim "untested" *(my reading)*. | `code_graph.py` docstring, G-F12 |
    19	| 11. Is F unused or dead? | No | grep, then run the tests. | 2,863 attribute, 927 dynamic and 84 name calls are unresolved, so a missing edge does not mean no caller. | G-F12 |
    20	| 12. Give me a map of the whole repo. | No | `PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md` and `INDEX.md`. | M18 forbids loading a whole-graph dump. This graph's dump is 2.6 MB. Graph responses also leave more context resident (67k vs 18k tokens on VS Code, external tool). | M18, G-F12, G-F7b, s3-F24 |
    21	| 13. A `covers:` path that isn't Python (docs, shell, JS) | No | git and grep. | The in-repo tool reads `*.py` only, and silence on other paths looks like "nothing found". | G-F18, code-graph §5 option B |
    22	| 14. A one-line comment changed in a large covered file: real change? | No | `git diff`, plus stdlib `ast` on that one file if precision is needed. | Option E gets the precision from the diff, without a repo-wide graph. | code-graph §5 option E |
    23	| 15. Is the graph I'm about to query current? | Don't check, rebuild whole. | Rebuild, or use git. | Drift cannot be cheaply detected, since detecting it costs the full rebuild. A cache is never patched. | G-F5c, G-F5 drift, R2, R3.1 |

    39	
    40	### What must be true first
    41	- **The trigger has fired.** Any one of three conditions makes the graph worth building (R5.3, code-graph §6):
    42	  - A `covers:` field names a function or class instead of a path.
    43	  - The check must answer about a file on no `covers:` list.
    44	  - Path-level flags raise too many false alarms. The rate is **EJ's number (OPEN)**.
    45	- **A demonstrated failure of the baseline** on this project's own tasks, as M21 requires. None is recorded.
    46	- **A wrapper that returns only the answer, never the dump** (M18). I found none. The tool has only `root`, `--exclude` and `--test-glob`, and its only consumer outside run folders is its own tests.
    47	- **A whole rebuild just before answering**, never a patch (R2, R3.1, G-F5 drift).
    48	- **The answer states its scope**, "Python only, lower bound", so a partial result is not read as complete (G-F18, G-F12).
    49	- **An explicit instruction to call it**, since agents with access often don't (G-F8, G-F20). How to word the instruction is model knowledge.
    50	- **A threshold for "grep took too many tool calls".** G-F7a shows the range (7 calls against 28–43) but no threshold for this repo. If one is needed, it is EJ's number.
    51

    59	- **Test suite time:** the cost of the full suite, `env -u NO_COLOR python3 -m pytest -q` (433 tests, G-F12), is GAP. So is any saving from graph-ordered tests.
    60	- **Git on long histories:** only 246 commits were measured. Cost at larger history is GAP.
    61	- **`INDEX.md` fields:** the Layout section of `RECOMMENDATION.md` says its lines carry `covers`, but `SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` says only "one line per active entry". If `covers` is not in `INDEX.md`, row 2 falls back to grep over `entries/`.
    62	- **Whether running `code_graph.py` ad hoc is gated by the R5 triggers:** `CACHES.md` gates the memory cache, not the tool. I treated the tool as ungated because it writes no file (G-F18). That is EJ's call.

   576	def answer_graph(route_input: RouteInput) -> dict:
   577	    started = time.perf_counter()
   578	    intent = graph_intent(route_input)
   579	    if intent is None:
   580	        return empty(time.perf_counter() - started,
   581	            note="no symbol and no file: the graph cannot answer this without one",
   582	        )
   583	    conn, problem = read_store("graph")
   584	    if conn is None:
   585	        return empty(time.perf_counter() - started, error=problem)
   586	
   587	    hits: list[str] = []
   588	    try:
   589	        if intent in ("callers-of-symbol", "tests-reaching-symbol", "where-symbol-is"):
   590	            targets = resolve_symbol(conn, route_input.symbol, route_input.file)
   591	            ids = [row[0] for row in targets]
   592	            if intent == "callers-of-symbol":
   593	                hits = [caller for caller, _callee in callers_of(conn, ids)]
   594	            elif intent == "tests-reaching-symbol":
   595	                hits = [test_id for test_id, _fn, _hops in tests_reaching(conn, ids)]
   596	            else:
   597	                hits = ids
   598	        else:  # tests-reaching-file, file-functions
   599	            file_rows = conn.execute(
   600	                "SELECT module_id FROM module_file WHERE file = ?", (route_input.file,)
   601	            ).fetchall()
   602	            ids = [row[0] for row in file_rows]
   603	            ids += [
   604	                row[0]
   605	                for row in conn.execute(
   606	                    "SELECT id FROM function WHERE file = ? ORDER BY line", (route_input.file,)
   607	                ).fetchall()
   608	            ]
   609	            if intent == "tests-reaching-file":
   610	                hits = [test_id for test_id, _fn, _hops in tests_reaching(conn, ids)]
   611	            else:
   612	                hits = ids
   613	    except sqlite3.Error as exc:
   614	        return empty(time.perf_counter() - started, error=f"graph query failed: {exc}")
   615	    finally:
   616	        conn.close()
   617	
   618	    unique: list[str] = []
   619	    for hit in hits:
   620	        if hit not in unique:
   621	            unique.append(hit)
   622	    unique = unique[:MAX_PATHS]
   623	    note = None
   624	    if not unique:
   625	        note = f"no graph answer for {route_input.symbol or route_input.file} ({intent})"
   626	    return {
   627	        "paths": unique,
   628	        "output": "\n".join(unique),
   629	        "seconds": round(time.perf_counter() - started, 6),
   630	        "intent": intent,
   631	        "hits_before_cap": len(set(hits)),
   632	        "note": note,
   633	    }
   634	
   635	
   636	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   637	# dispatch
   638	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
   639	
   640	_HANDLERS = {
   641	    "rg": answer_rg,
   642	    "fts": answer_fts,

   123	def find_symbol(repo: Path, path: str, name: str):
   124	    """The `ast` node defining `name` (dotted for a method) in a Python file, or None.
   125	
   126	    Read from the file, not from a store, so the symbol-level score does not
   127	    depend on how a store chunked the file.  A def, a class, or (for the last
   128	    part of the name) a module- or class-level assignment.
   129	    """
   130	    try:
   131	        tree = ast.parse((repo / path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   132	    except (OSError, SyntaxError, ValueError):
   133	        return None
   134	    body, found = tree.body, None
   135	    parts = name.split(".")
   136	    for depth, part in enumerate(parts):
   137	        last = depth == len(parts) - 1
   138	        found = None
   139	        for node in body:
   140	            if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef, ast.ClassDef)):
   141	                if node.name == part:
   142	                    found = node
   143	            elif last and isinstance(node, (ast.Assign, ast.AnnAssign)):
   144	                targets = node.targets if isinstance(node, ast.Assign) else [node.target]
   145	                if any(isinstance(t, ast.Name) and t.id == part for t in targets):
   146	                    found = node
   147	        if found is None:
   148	            return None
   149	        body = getattr(found, "body", [])
   150	    return found
   151	
   152	
   153	def symbol_span(node) -> tuple[int, int]:
   154	    """First and last line of a symbol's node, decorators included."""
   155	    first = min([node.lineno] + [d.lineno for d in getattr(node, "decorator_list", [])])
   156	    return first, node.end_lineno or node.lineno
   157	
   158	
   159	def printed_lines(output: str, path: str) -> list[int]:
   160	    """Line numbers of the rg match lines printed for `path`."""
   161	    pattern = re.compile(re.escape(path) + r":(\d+):")
   162	    return [int(m.group(1)) for m in (pattern.match(line) for line in output.splitlines()) if m]
   163	
   164	
   165	def symbol_hit(found: str, want: str, route: str, output: str, repo: Path) -> bool:
   166	    """Does one returned entry hit one gold entry at the symbol level?
   167	
   168	    A gold with no `::` is a file, scored as at file level.  For a gold
   169	    `path::name` the entry must be in that file and:
   170	    * `rg` (paths only): a printed match line of that file lies inside the
   171	      symbol's lines;
   172	    * a `path::X` entry: X is the symbol, the top-level def/class holding it, or
   173	      a method of it (graph ids nest with `::`, read here as `.`);
   174	    * a bare `path` entry (a module-code chunk or a graph module): the symbol is
   175	      module-level code.
   176	    """
   177	    want_path, _, want_name = want.partition("::")
   178	    found_path, _, found_name = found.partition("::")
   179	    if found_path != want_path:
   180	        return False
   181	    if not want_name:
   182	        return True
   183	    node = find_symbol(repo, want_path, want_name)
   184	    if node is None:
   185	        return False
   186	    if route == "rg":
   187	        first, last = symbol_span(node)
   188	        return any(first <= n <= last for n in printed_lines(output, want_path))
   189	    if found_name:
   190	        found_name = found_name.replace("::", ".")
   191	        return (
   192	            found_name == want_name
   193	            or found_name.startswith(want_name + ".")
   194	            or want_name.startswith(found_name + ".")
   195	        )
   196	    return "." not in want_name and isinstance(node, (ast.Assign, ast.AnnAssign))
   197	
   198	
   549	            meta = builder.read_meta(builder.store_path(store))
   550	            report["git_head"] = meta.get("git_head")
   551	            report["newest_source_mtime"] = meta.get("newest_source_mtime")
   552	            builds.append(report)
   553	
   554	    model_started = time.perf_counter()
   555	    router.embedder()  # loaded once, so a route's seconds are its own work
   556	    model_seconds = round(time.perf_counter() - model_started, 3)
   557	
   558	    started = time.perf_counter()
   559	    results = run_questions(questions, repo, keep_output=args.keep_output)
   560	    run_seconds = round(time.perf_counter() - started, 3)

    60	def corpus_paths(repo: Path) -> list[str]:
    61	    """Repo-relative paths of the corpus, sorted, excludes applied.
    62	
    63	    `git ls-files` decides membership, so untracked scratch never enters a store.
    64	    """
    65	    out = subprocess.run(
    66	        ["git", "-C", str(repo), "ls-files", *CORPUS_GLOBS],
    67	        check=True,
    68	        capture_output=True,
    69	        text=True,
    70	    ).stdout
    71	    kept = [
    72	        line.strip()
    73	        for line in out.splitlines()
    74	        if line.strip() and not line.startswith(EXCLUDED_PREFIXES)
    75	    ]
    76	    return sorted(kept)

    84	def markdown_chunks(path: str, lines: list[str]) -> list[Chunk]:
    85	    """One chunk per heading section, plus the preamble above the first heading."""
    86	    starts = []
    87	    for index, line in enumerate(lines):

    92	        return [Chunk(path, 1, len(lines), "\n".join(lines))] if lines else []

   274	def run_code_graph(repo: Path) -> dict:
   275	    """code_graph.py's JSON, read from stdout.
   276	
   277	    The script exits 1 when it logged a read or parse error, so a non-zero exit
   278	    with usable JSON is reported rather than thrown away.
   279	    """

   281	    for pattern in GRAPH_EXCLUDES:
   282	        command += ["--exclude", pattern]
   283	    done = subprocess.run(command, capture_output=True, text=True)
   284	    if not done.stdout.strip():
   285	        raise RuntimeError(f"code_graph.py printed nothing: {done.stderr.strip()[:400]}")
   286	    return json.loads(done.stdout)

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""chunk.py -- split the repo corpus into the chunks every store is built from.
     3	
     4	The corpus is `git ls-files '*.py' '*.md'` minus the two run folders the brief
     5	excludes.  Two languages, two rules:
     6	
     7	* Markdown splits by heading section.  The lines above the first heading (the
     8	  preamble) are their own chunk; a file with no heading at all is one chunk.
     9	* Python splits by top-level `def` or `class`, decorators included, and the
    10	  module-level code -- every line outside those blocks -- is one more chunk.
    11	
    12	Each chunk carries `path`, `start_line`, `end_line` and `text`.  It also carries
    13	`name`, the top-level symbol or `None`, and `kind`.  `name` is what lets a route
    14	answer with `path::name` for a function and with `path` for a document; a
    15	markdown chunk never gets one, so a file is one answer no matter how many
    16	sections it has.
    17	
    18	Used by build.py to build, and by nothing at query time: a route reads a store,
    19	never the corpus.  Run it directly to see what the corpus looks like.
    20	"""
    21	
    22	from __future__ import annotations
    23	
    24	import argparse
    25	import ast
    26	import json
    27	import re
    28	import subprocess
    29	import sys
    30	from dataclasses import dataclass, asdict
    31	from pathlib import Path
    32	
    33	EXCLUDED_PREFIXES = (
    34	    "runs/20261012-caches-routing/",
    35	    "runs/20261012-memory-caches/",
    36	)
    37	
    38	CORPUS_GLOBS = ("*.py", "*.md")
    39	
    40	HEADING_RE = re.compile(r"^(#{1,6})[ \t]+(\S.*?)[ \t]*$")
    41	
    42	TOP_LEVEL_DEFS = (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef, ast.ClassDef)
    43	
   370	          "mean_sym_hit@1": 0.0,
   371	          "mean_sym_hit@10": 0.5,
   372	          "mean_sym_hit@5": 0.5,
   373	          "questions": 6
   374	        }
   375	      }
   376	    }
   377	  },

   283	def compare_paths(first: list[dict], second: list[dict]) -> dict:
   284	    """Where two runs disagree, answer for answer."""
   285	    index = {(row["id"], row["route"]): row for row in first if row.get("applicable")}
   286	    other = {(row["id"], row["route"]): row for row in second if row.get("applicable")}
   287	    mismatches = []
   288	    for key in sorted(set(index) | set(other)):
   289	        here = index.get(key, {}).get("paths", [])
   290	        there = other.get(key, {}).get("paths", [])
   291	        if here != there:
   292	            mismatches.append(
   293	                {"id": key[0], "route": key[1], "first": here, "second": there}
   294	            )
   540	
   541	
   542	def tests_reaching(conn: sqlite3.Connection, function_ids: list[str]) -> list[tuple]:
   543	    """Tests that reach any of these functions, nearest first.
   544	
   545	    `hops` is the shortest number of call and fixture edges, so a test that
   546	    calls the function is ranked above one that reaches it through two hops.
   547	    """
   548	    if not function_ids:
   549	        return []
   550	    marks = ",".join("?" * len(function_ids))
   551	    return conn.execute(
   552	        f"SELECT r.test_id, r.function_id, min(r.hops) AS hops"
   553	        f"  FROM test_reach AS r"
   554	        f"  JOIN function AS f ON f.id = r.test_id"
   555	        f" WHERE r.function_id IN ({marks})"
   556	        f" GROUP BY r.test_id, r.function_id"
   557	        f" ORDER BY hops, r.test_id",
   558	        function_ids,
   559	    ).fetchall()
   560	
   561	
   562	def callers_of(conn: sqlite3.Connection, function_ids: list[str]) -> list[tuple]:
   563	    """Callers of these functions: (caller, callee), functions before modules."""
   564	    if not function_ids:
   565	        return []
   566	    marks = ",".join("?" * len(function_ids))
   567	    return conn.execute(
   568	        f"SELECT c.caller, c.callee FROM call_edge AS c"
   569	        f" LEFT JOIN function AS f ON f.id = c.caller"
   570	        f" WHERE c.callee IN ({marks})"
   571	        f" ORDER BY (f.id IS NULL), c.caller",
   572	        function_ids,
   573	    ).fetchall()

codex
The source checks are complete. The review will distinguish printed-output measurements from unmeasured token savings, and routing guidance from demonstrated correctness. I also found a coverage gap: the benchmark asks about pytest configuration in `pyproject.toml`, but excludes `.toml` files from every search route.
codex
### Strengths of A

- **My reading:** A is the better starting point for memory-entry routing. Its table covers discovery, exact identifiers, completeness, absence, lifecycle links, rationale, drift, resumption and non-Python paths (A:56–72). B disperses that guidance across its per-store explanations.
- A includes concrete acceptance criteria for disposal, rebuilding, missing caches, source attribution and triggers (A:340–394). These make the design more reviewable, although several criteria need correction below.
- A preserves disagreements and acknowledges that the code-graph contributor coordinated with another agent. The evidence record explicitly distinguishes disagreement from corroboration. [DECISION-RULES.md:21–35, 132–164](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-enhance/DECISION-RULES.md:21)

### Strengths of B

- **My reading:** B foregrounds the development/testing questions the user actually wants answered: definitions, callers, affected tests, rationale, configuration and concepts (B:93–102). A contains the same development table and benchmark section.
- The principal benchmark figures match the saved results: category scores, output means, rebuild times and the 14.68 ms maximum. Both versions disclose unequal output formats, exclusion of misses and the absence of an end-to-end agent trial. [results.md:13–28, 30–102, 276](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/bench/results.md:13)
- B clearly leaves the proposed expansion from memory retrieval to code/docs retrieval to EJ (B:111–114). The proposal file is explicitly for acceptance, amendment or rejection. [vision-proposals.md:3–6](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/vision-proposals.md:3)
- **Evidence strength, shared by both:** Codex authored the questions and initial gold; five Claude agents checked the categories; opencode authored the harness; Claude audited and repaired it. This is one repository and one 40-question set, with 6–10 questions per category, four runtime-recall questions and three subprocess questions—not independent replications. The repaired harness and revised prose were not re-reviewed. [routing/state.md:37–60, 67–76](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/state.md:37)

### Errors and weaknesses

- **A:262 — high.** A replaces EJ’s vector threshold with a measurement-decided comparison; A:276 and R5 repeat that change. The cited comparison method is a **draft proposal**, while the settled vision says all three trigger numbers belong to EJ. A:324 simultaneously retains the old ownership statement. Sources: [enhance/SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md:3, 165–180](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-enhance/SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md:165), [vision:33](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/docs/memory-system/vision-statements/caches-memory-vision-statements.md:33). Measurements can inform EJ’s decision; they cannot silently replace it.

- **A:43 — medium.** “A cache is never the answer to a question the files answer” would exclude every legitimate derived cache: its information necessarily comes from files. The vision requires files to remain authoritative and readable without caches; it does not prohibit cheaper retrieval of the same information. Source: [vision:25–29, 34](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/docs/memory-system/vision-statements/caches-memory-vision-statements.md:25).

- **A:18 — medium.** The claimed blind verification “on this file’s text” overstates the recorded check. The verifier examined `DECISION-RULES.md`, and found the planted alteration partly by diffing against the clean copy. That does not independently validate the final A document or its later threshold changes. Source: [enhance/run-report.md:15–17, 28–39](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-enhance/run-report.md:28).

- **A:75 — medium.** The paragraph mixes measured timings with unsupported costs. The 226K-token figure belongs to a 1,144-document enterprise benchmark, not the synthetic 10,000-entry grep test; “opening every hit” is not its established mechanism. The claimed `INDEX.md` scan under 0.01 s also has no measurement. Sources: [S-F4:55–60 and S-F13:98–107](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-memory-caches/nodes/sqlite-fts.claude.md:55), [DECISION-RULES.md:149–151, 191–193](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-enhance/DECISION-RULES.md:149). The primary paper confirms the token figures but describes accumulated model usage with bounded search/read tools. [BM25 paper, §4.4 and Appendix C.2](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.26497v3)

- **A:90 — low.** Gitignored does not mean absent from disk. Three SQLite files currently exist under `runs/20261012-caches-routing/bench/.cache/`. The benchmark record itself names those persistent files. Source: [bench/README.md:40](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/bench/README.md:40). The inherited recon explanation is wrong.

- **A:197; B:124 — high.** Comparing only the newest source mtime cannot guarantee “stale never answers.” **My counterexample:** delete an indexed entry without making a surviving file newer than the cache; the condition never rebuilds, and the deleted entry remains searchable. Preserved timestamps create another failure. The source records this heuristic, alongside content-hash alternatives, but establishes no stale-answer guarantee. Source: [S-F9:80 and S-F15:115–120](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-memory-caches/nodes/sqlite-fts.claude.md:115). A:352–357 elevates the incomplete heuristic into acceptance criteria.

- **A:67; B:216 — high.** Last modification of an entry is not its last verification against code. An unrelated entry edit can hide code changes since verification. The cited memory requirement explicitly binds staleness to the **verified revision**, and describes diffing covered paths from that revision. Source: [RECOMMENDATION.md:44, 194–198](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261011-project-memory-engines/RECOMMENDATION.md:194). Git is the right substrate; this comparison is the wrong baseline for the stated verification question.

- **A:124; B:51 — medium.** The table calls the character metric “first right answer,” although it stops at the first line naming a gold **file**. Later statements that the caches “save reading” also exceed what was measured. The caveats are good but contradict these summaries. Source: [results.md:26–28](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/bench/results.md:26). This is evidence about printed output, not answer completion, agent tokens or development time saved.

- **A:138; B:65 — medium.** BM-7 has the wrong unit and aggregation description. The coverage reducer produces **test-file sets**; 0.78 is the mean of four question-level recalls, not the overall share of individual tests found. The recorded file counts imply graph recall of 8/13 across question/file instances, versus 4/13 for grep/FTS/hybrid. Sources: [derive_maps.py:1–3, 22–32](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/cov/derive_maps.py:1), [runtime-recall.json:2–109](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/nodes/runtime-recall.json:2). Keep 0.78, but label it macro-average test-file recall, n=4.

- **A:141; B:68 — medium.** “Grep found 1 of 3” needs a completeness qualifier. Grep hits C06 and C07, but retrieves only half C07’s gold files. Thus it finds something relevant for 2/3 questions and a complete gold set for 1/3. Source: [results.md:200–214](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/bench/results.md:200).

- **A:153; B:80 — medium.** The experiment is described as testing “the vector index” without identifying its embedding model. It tests `minishlab/potion-base-8M`, a static Model2Vec model. Those results do not establish the performance of the alternative contextual embeddings discussed later. Sources: [build.py:50–51](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/bench/build.py:50), [V-F18:206–220](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-memory-caches/nodes/vector.claude.md:206).

- **A:173; B:100 — medium.** Configuration coverage has an undisclosed benchmark defect: E02’s gold answer is `pyproject.toml`, but all search routes are restricted to `.py` and `.md`. Its failure is therefore partly corpus exclusion, not retrieval quality. Sources: [queries.json:681–695](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/queries.json:681), [chunk.py:4, 38](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/bench/chunk.py:38). An actual agent also needs a manifest/config-file route.

- **A:168; B:95 — medium.** The development table omits the graph’s Python-only scope and the warning that a nonempty caller/test result can still be incomplete. Falling back only on an empty answer does not address missing edges in a nonempty answer. Sources: [G-F12:119–120](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-memory-caches/nodes/code-graph.opencode.stdout.md:119), [runtime-recall.json:90–109](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/nodes/runtime-recall.json:90). Put these limits in the lookup table.

- **A:177; B:109 — medium.** Saying R1–R8 apply unchanged does not resolve their mismatch with code/docs retrieval. A’s R5.1 requires an overflowing memory index; R6 requires active-entry status and `covers`; R7 requires an entry filename. These cannot apply literally to arbitrary code nodes and documentation chunks. Source: [A:370–387](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-merge/inputs/version-A.md:370). B additionally references R1–R8 without including or directly linking their definitions.

- **A:265; B:191 — medium.** “The positive results found combine vectors with keyword search” is too broad even for memory-style text. V-F3 reports vector-only outperforming grep in five of ten file-delivery harness/model pairs. Hybrid-only can remain a proposed policy, but the evidence does not compel it. Source: [V-F3:60–68](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-memory-caches/nodes/vector.claude.md:60).

- **A:66 — low.** The rationale row cites `s3-F4`, which concerns StateTape’s staleness maintenance. The relevant rationale finding is `s3-F21`, also used by A’s underlying decision record. Sources: [research INDEX.md:119, 136](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/docs/research/20261011-agent-memory-graph-r2/INDEX.md:119), [DECISION-RULES.md:52](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-enhance/DECISION-RULES.md:52).

### Conflicts between A and B

- **Who decides thresholds.** A:262–276 substitutes measured cost comparisons; B:189–202 retains EJ’s chosen miss count. **B is better supported:** the settled vision reserves the numbers for EJ, and V-F13 explicitly calls the threshold EJ’s rather than a research finding. A’s comparison method is useful as a proposal. [vision:33](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/docs/memory-system/vision-statements/caches-memory-vision-statements.md:33), [V-F13:165–168](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-memory-caches/nodes/vector.claude.md:165).

- **Graph for exact lookup.** A:53 says the graph is only for multi-hop questions, and A:62 routes exact identifiers to files. B:95–97 proposes graph lookup for definitions and callers. A’s later table repeats B, so this is also an internal conflict in A. **B’s proposal is better supported for finding Python candidates in this experiment:** graph and grep tie at 0.75 on exact identifiers, with smaller graph output. **A’s caution is better supported for exhaustive call sites and absence claims.** These are different question contracts and need separate rows. [results.md:34–40, 215–229](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/bench/results.md:34), [G-F15:58–60](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-memory-caches/nodes/code-graph.opencode.stdout.md:58).

- **Finding rationale versus supplying rationale.** A:66 says no cache for “why”; B:99 proposes full-text to locate the relevant documentation. **B is better supported for discovery; A for answering:** BM-4 measures retrieval hits, while the memory design requires reading the decision and evidence before relying on it. [results.md:68–78](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/runs/20261012-caches-routing/bench/results.md:68), [SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md:114–117](/home/smoke01/dev/ancient-games/docs/memory-system/SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md:114).

The benchmark sections otherwise agree and derive from the same run. Their agreement provides no additional independent evidence.

### Proposed merged outline

1. **Status, scope and EJ’s pending decisions — combine both.** Link the settled vision; distinguish current memory scope from proposed code/docs expansion. Keep trigger ownership explicit.
2. **One question-to-route table — combine both, with new text.** Use A’s memory coverage and B’s development rows. Include corpus, prerequisite, route, fallback and answer limitation in each row. Add configuration files, function behaviour, exhaustive callers and dead-code questions.
3. **Shared never-rules — take from A, corrected.** Preserve disposal, full rebuild, on-demand reads and authoritative files. Add a checkable session-start prohibition and a freshness contract covering deletion and model changes.
4. **Build triggers — combine both.** Keep B’s EJ-owned thresholds; present A’s measurement comparisons as proposed evidence for EJ. Define code/docs triggers only through an explicit vision proposal.
5. **Three store designs — combine both.** Scope active-entry filters to memory, identify graph language coverage, and specify consistent publication of FTS/vector rebuilds.
6. **Development/testing evidence — take from B, corrected.** Name the embedding model, metric units, corpus exclusions, cold-start costs and exact sample sizes. Retain the full-suite safeguard.
7. **Acceptance criteria — take from A, corrected.** Separate common cache criteria from memory-specific and proposed code/docs criteria.
8. **Evidence provenance and unresolved questions — combine both.** Name each check’s author, distinguish self-checks from independent checks, and preserve unmeasured outcomes and pending EJ decisions.

- I did not rebuild caches, rerun benchmarks or execute tests.
